## competitors
SUMMARY: The Indian JEE/NEET test-prep market in 2026 is a consolidating, margin-compressed oligopoly sitting on a genuinely enormous top of funnel: NEET UG 2026 had 22,79,743 registrations and JEE Main 2026 had 16,04,854 (15,38,468 unique appeared). Only Physics Wallah is growing — FY25 revenue ₹2,886.64 Cr (+49%), 4.5M paid users, IPO'd 18 Nov 2025 at ₹109 and listed ~33% up — while Allen's revenue shrank to ₹3,067 Cr with net profit collapsing ~70% to ₹41 Cr, Vedantu did just ₹227 Cr on a ₹210 Cr pre-tax loss, and Unacademy was absorbed by upGrad in a March 2026 all-share deal at sub-$500M vs a $3.5B peak. Its own CEO conceded "the sector itself has not seen enough real product innovation in recent years" — which is the market opening. The single biggest 2026 event is the NEET paper leak: the 3 May 2026 exam (2.27M candidates) was cancelled on 12 May after 120–140 of 180 questions matched a leaked guess paper, a re-exam ran 21 June, and the Education Minister has committed NEET to computer-based testing from 2027 — making NTA-identical CBT mock UI, integrity/proctoring, and anti-leak provenance table stakes rather than nice-to-haves. Incumbent apps are content-delivery businesses with test engines bolted on: their differentiation is faculty brand and video, and their 1–2★ reviews are overwhelmingly about engineering and sales conduct, not pedagogy — constant crashes, broken login/session, laggy PDF viewers, no iPad landscape, missing lectures, aggressive spam calls, and non-refundable subscriptions. The genuinely good product work is happening at the free/cheap edge: MARKS (1M+ downloads, 4.8★, 1 lakh+ PYQs, free, but Android-only with no native iOS app) and Quizrr/MathonGo (~900 tests, NTA-clone interface, 15-page analysis, per-question time analytics, predicted percentile, video solutions). Almost nothing in India ships what UWorld and Anki have proven: illustrated per-option rationales, tutor-vs-timed modes, a persistent searchable notebook, auto-generated spaced-repetition cards from wrong answers (FSRS is free, open source, and default in Anki since 23.10, cutting reviews 20–30% for the same retention), and peer percentile at the topic level. Duolingo's retention primitives (7+ day streak users retain at 2.4x; streak freeze cut at-risk churn 21%; leagues lifted lesson completion 25%) are copied superficially in India as coins and leaderboards without the underlying loss-aversion and cohort design.
FINDINGS:
- (high) TAM is verified and very large; funnel is exam-cycle-synchronised: NEET UG 2026: 22,79,743 registered (13,32,928 female / 9,46,815 male, ~58% women). JEE Main 2026: 16,04,854 registered, 15,38,468 unique candidates appeared across both sessions; category split 6,11,953 OBC-NCL, 5,38,239 General, 1,80,086 EWS, 1,56,038 SC, 52,152 ST. India test-prep market valued USD 11.60B in 2025, ~8.7% CAGR 2026-2030 (Technavio); IMARC's narrower online-coaching figure projects USD 1,998.4M by 2034 at 15.89% — the two numbers measure different scopes, treat with caution. => IMPLICATION: A 10,000-concurrent target is ~0.25% of the NEET cohort — realistic. But load is brutally spiky: it clusters around scheduled mock windows, result days, and Jan/Apr/May exam months. Design Supabase/Redis capacity for burst, not average, and build the leaderboard/rank computation as a batch+cache job, not a live aggregate query.
- (high) NEET 2026 paper leak forces NEET to CBT from 2027 — the single most important product signal: 3 May 2026 NEET UG held for 2.27M candidates; a WhatsApp-circulated 'guess paper' matched 120–140 of 180 questions (Chemistry/Biology heaviest). NTA cancelled the exam 12 May 2026, CBI took over the same day, re-exam held 21 June 2026 (question papers moved by Indian Air Force aircraft; setters sequestered). Results 16 July 2026: 110,000 qualified, 58% women, 270,000+ skipped the retest, attendance fell 96.72% → 87.72%. Arrests included NTA translators and subject experts; students paid ₹2–10 lakh for access. CBI found the 2025 paper was compromised by the same network. Education Minister Dharmendra Pradhan announced NEET moves to computer-based testing from 2027; Public Examinations (Prevention of Unfair Means) Act 2024 to be amended with fast-track courts and >10-year maximum sentences. At least 12 aspirant suicides linked. => IMPLICATION: From 2027 NEET students will practise for a CBT they have never sat. Whoever ships the most faithful NTA CBT clone (question palette colour states, section lock, mark-for-review, on-screen calculator absence, per-section timer, submit confirmation) owns NEET mock share in 2026-27. Also: content provenance and anti-leak posture become marketing assets — signed/watermarked question delivery, no bulk export, per-student randomisation are defensible claims, not just engineering hygiene.
- (high) Physics Wallah: only major player growing; now public and therefore disclosure-rich: FY23 ₹744.32 Cr → FY24 ₹1,940.71 Cr → FY25 ₹2,886.64 Cr revenue (+49% FY25). Net loss narrowed from ₹1,131.13 Cr (FY24) to ₹243.26 Cr (FY25); EBITDA positive. 4.5M (0.45 crore) paid users FY25, 59.19% CAGR FY23-25. Revenue mix: online 48.6%, offline centres 46.8%. IPO bid 11–13 Nov 2025, price band ₹103–109, issue price ₹109, listed NSE/BSE 18 Nov 2025 at ~₹143–145 (~33% premium). iOS app v10.1 (20 Jul 2026), 428.81 MB, 4.31★ / 14,432 ratings, iOS 14.1+. Claims 3.5M registered students, 78 lakh YouTube subscribers, 100+ cities of Vidyapeeth/Pathshala centres. Online course prices ₹3,199–₹99,989; JEE online batches commonly ₹2,000–₹5,000; PWNSAT scholarship test up to 100% waiver. => IMPLICATION: PW sets the price floor: ₹2,000–5,000/year for a full online batch. Any paid tier above ~₹1,500/year for practice-only must justify itself against PW's bundled video+test+doubt. The 428 MB binary and heavy video payload is also a weakness you can attack — a lean, fast, practice-first app is a genuine differentiator on low-end Android.
- (high) Allen: revenue shrinking, profit down ~70%, digital arm growing off a small base: FY25 operating revenue ₹3,067 Cr (down from ₹3,245 Cr FY24); total revenue ₹3,307 Cr incl. ₹240 Cr other income; net profit ₹41 Cr, down ~70% from ~₹136 Cr. Net cash position ~₹2,000 Cr. 300+ classroom campuses across 70 cities, 3.5 lakh+ offline students/yr. Allen Digital grew 40%+ YoY in FY25; acquired Doubtnut in 2023 (reported ~$10M, after Doubtnut rejected a ~$150M Byju's offer). Allen iOS app 4.16★ / 1,667 ratings, v4.11.2 (21 Jul 2026), 185.96 MB. Test series structure: JEE Main Target 2026/27 ~19 tests (10 minor, 2 review, 2 semi-major, major full-syllabus); JEE Main+Advanced ~25 papers; NEET ~19 tests (11 CBT + 8 pen-and-paper). Allen Digital Online Practice bundles Custom Practice, Improvement Book, Revision Notes, Flash Card, AI Doubt Support. ALLEN JEE test series widely listed at ₹999 (2025-26; unverified on Allen's own site — the allen.in product pages are client-rendered and return no pricing to fetchers). => IMPLICATION: Allen already ships the exact feature vocabulary you plan (custom practice, improvement book, flashcards) — so those are table stakes, not differentiators. Their weakness is execution quality on mobile (1,667 iOS ratings vs PW's 14,432 despite a far larger student base) and a still-hybrid pen-and-paper test series. Note their 'Improvement Book' concept: an auto-collected wrong-answer book. Copy it and do it better.
- (high) Unacademy absorbed by upGrad in March 2026 — the clearest evidence of product stagnation: 15 Mar 2026: 100% share-swap acquisition announced (reported ~$218M / ₹2,055 Cr). Valuation fell below $500M in Dec 2025 vs $3.5B peak (Aug 2021), ~85-90% wipeout. Total funding raised $854.3M over 13 rounds; >$100M cash on hand; ₹500M (~$5.4M) ESOP buyback with ~40% participation. CY2026 revenue target ~₹400 Cr. Gaurav Munjal: "Unacademy helped invent the modern edtech playbook. Along the way we lost some focus and market share, and the sector itself has not seen enough real product innovation in recent years." Earlier: >1,000 layoffs across rounds; Allen's ~$800M acquisition attempt collapsed in 2024. iOS Learner app 4.49★ / 54,284 ratings but last updated 10 Jul 2025 — ~12 months stale as of Jul 2026. Educator app 3.33★ / 391 ratings, last updated 11 Jan 2024. => IMPLICATION: Two things to steal: (1) the market leader's own CEO says there is no product innovation — that is your positioning line; (2) the Educator app at 3.33★ and 2.5 years without an update proves teacher-side tooling is universally neglected. A genuinely good ADMIN authoring experience (LaTeX-first, bulk import, image OCR, versioning, analytics) is an unclaimed wedge — it is how you win institutional/coaching-centre deals, not just B2C.
- (high) Verbatim 1-2★ complaints, Allen iOS: crashes, broken auth, PDF viewer, no iPad landscape: 1★ 'Dost' by Rj bihari (05/08/2025): "one of the worst educational apps I've ever used...crashes constantly...login system is broken...PDF viewer lags." 4★ Soumil Ghosh Dastidar (12 Jan 2026) lists six defects: buggy PDF viewer, poor lecture organization, slow doubt resolution, misaligned difficulty labelling in practice questions, login crashes, no iPad landscape support. 'sophiabm' (08/04/2025): "duplicate connection" error 4-5 times per class despite a single device. 'Vihaan K.' (20 May 2026): landscape breaks layout on iPad Gen 11 and portrait fails after rotation. 'Aviral.49' (24 Jan 2026): microphone interruption minimises full-screen; live sessions lack playback controls for late joiners. Overall 4.2★ / 1.7k iOS ratings. => IMPLICATION: Concrete, cheap engineering wins for your app: (1) session/auth must survive token refresh and multi-device without a 'duplicate connection' kill — implement a proper Supabase session strategy with graceful device handoff, not hard eviction; (2) ship a native, GPU-accelerated PDF/image viewer with pinch-zoom, highlight, and resume-position; (3) support landscape + iPad/tablet from day one (RN + Expo makes this an explicit choice, not a default). These three defects appear in nearly every competitor's reviews.
- (high) Verbatim 1-2★ complaints, PW iOS: platform parity gaps and streaming failures: Reported: Community tab, Saarthi (doubt) feature and Infinite Mentorship missing on iPad despite paid subscription; "lecture stop directly with having full internet"; downloads very slow even on 5G; poll timers expire before students can answer; requests to "upgrade the video streaming quality to at least a stable 1080p HD"; doubt submission fails with error 'dRoomId is null or undefined'; 'explore free batches' section renders only a broken image icon; 'My batches' described as a nightmare; lectures not uploaded with no in-app notice. Content complaints: 1★-flavoured 'Lacks quality' (WizenedPsychic, 10/09/2024) — "They don't teach basics and jump directly into advanced concepts", teachers "get mad if we ask doubts"; 'Unprofessional Teaching and Discriminatory Behavior at PW Offline Centre' (chalna_mkl, 07/07/2025). Overall 4.3★ / ~14k iOS ratings. => IMPLICATION: Feature parity across iOS/Android/tablet is a real, recurring paid-user grievance at the market leader. With React Native + Expo you get parity nearly free — make it an explicit marketing claim. Also: never show a silent empty state. Every failed fetch needs a typed error with a retry and a status message ('solutions for this test are being uploaded, ETA X'). PW loses trust mostly through unexplained absence, not through errors.
- (high) Verbatim 1-2★ complaints, Unacademy + Testbook: the complaints are about SALES, not the product: Unacademy iOS: 'Worst experience' (Anup12396, 12/02/2025) — "10-20 calls weekly for purchases but impossible to reach customer support"; 'Bakwas unreasonable spam calls everyday' (Satya9707, 20/02/2025) — "continuous calls regardless of studying or meetings...pathetic tech team"; 'Its a big fraud' (Sachin githala, 22/06/2023) — "no response after 4 days despite promised 10-12 hour reply time"; 'Fraud company …. Cheaters' (Anshuman69, 30/09/2023) — "half lectures missing...no response to emails after 8-10 days"; 'Unacademy is good, this app is ugh' — "recordings get stuck when on 1.25x+ speeds". Testbook iOS (4.6★ / 22k): 'Don't fall into this trap' (Jan 2026) — "money is non refundable and non cancellation policy, guyz its a scam and nothing else", "they will call you again and again to buy the subscription and apply a mandate"; 'Don't listen' (Oct 2025) — "60% in English and 40% in Hindi, which I can't understand", "platform clearly refusing to refund"; 'Rude behaviour' — "talked to me very rudely saying why are you watching our videos again and again"; 'Fraud and Scammer' (28/07/2025) — "Outdated, Pre-recorded Content", "Broken Refund Promises...No refund. No response." => IMPLICATION: The most reliably hated thing in Indian test prep is the telesales + auto-mandate + no-refund pattern. A self-serve, no-phone-number, transparent-refund product is a differentiated brand position with zero engineering cost. Do not collect phone numbers as a required signup field if you can avoid it; if you must (OTP auth), state explicitly in-app that you will not call. Also: language mixing in content is a specific, repeated complaint — tag every asset with a language and let students filter hard.
- (high) MARKS (getmarks.app) is the strongest free practice product — and it has no native iOS app: 1,000,000+ downloads, 4.8★ on Play Store, 40,000+ reviews, 2,000,000+ questions solved daily. 1 lakh+ PYQs, chapter-wise, across JEE Main, JEE Advanced, NEET, BITSAT, WBJEE, MHT CET, NDA, KVPY. Features: custom test creation, quiz mode, formula cards, weak-area tracking, chapter-wise mocks in exact NTA CBT format, daily practice challenges with 10-50 question targets, all-India leaderboard, badges for top performers, and a distraction-free web version (Chrome/Edge/Safari). 100% free, no premium tier. Confirmed absent from the Apple App Store India catalogue in a July 2026 iTunes Search API query — iOS is served only as a web app. => IMPLICATION: MARKS is your closest functional competitor and your benchmark for free-tier scope: PYQ bank + custom test builder + CBT-accurate engine + daily challenge + leaderboard must all be free or you will not acquire. Their iOS absence and their zero-monetisation stance are the two openings: ship a first-class iOS app, and monetise via things MARKS structurally cannot (spaced repetition on your own errors, video solutions, teacher-graded rich-text/LaTeX solutions, sectioned timed mocks with server-authoritative timing).
- (high) Quizrr (powered by MathonGo) is the best analytics product in Indian JEE prep: ~900 tests including chapter-wise, part tests and full tests, built to the NTA reduced syllabus. Headline feature is a "Most Detailed 15 page Test Analysis" covering: chapter-wise and test-wise mistake categorisation, time-spent analysis per question, predicted percentile, and difficulty/weightage patterns. Also: real NTA exam interface simulation, video solutions for every question, an in-product notebook section, and '11 more Advanced Features'. Scholarship/refund: ₹6,000 refunded to students scoring 99 percentile or above in JEE Main 2026, marketed as '100% Money Back'. Pricing is not disclosed on the public landing pages (FAQ questions exist with no rendered answers) — unverified. MathonGo separately bundles a chapter-wise + full test series 'worth INR 3,990' free with its JEE Advanced 2026 crash course. => IMPLICATION: Per-question time-spent analysis and mistake-type categorisation are the two analytics that students actually act on, and only Quizrr does them well. Your analytics should ship: time-per-question vs cohort median, an 'overtime' flag, mistake taxonomy (conceptual / silly / calculation / guessed-right / did-not-attempt), predicted percentile from cohort scores, and chapter weightage vs your accuracy as a 2D scatter. This is the highest-leverage differentiator available at low engineering cost because you already control the test engine.
- (medium) Test-series pricing benchmarks across the market (INR): eSaral 'India's 1st AI-Powered JEE Main+Advanced 2026 Test Series': 200 tests, MRP ₹12,000, offer ₹2,000; platform also claims 30,000+ practice questions, 250+ mock tests, AI performance reports, strength-weakness radar, personalised improvement plans, 5-layered doubt solving. Aakash: Final Test Series NEET 2026 ₹10,000; Full Syllabus 'M' Test Series NEET 2026 ₹7,000; MTS for JEE Main 2026 ₹3,500; AIATS = 39 NEET-pattern part-syllabus + mock tests with iTutor recorded videos, live class notes, e-materials, and interactive Test Discussion classes. ALLEN JEE test series listed at ₹999 (2025-26, third-party source, unverified). MathonGo test series 'worth ₹3,990'. PW online batches ₹2,000–₹5,000 typical, full range ₹3,199–₹99,989. ALLEN TALLENTEX 2026 registration ₹300 non-refundable, against a ₹250 Cr scholarship pool (up to 90% fee waiver) and ₹1.25 Cr in cash prizes, classes 5-10, offline 5 & 12 Oct 2025 / online 5, 7, 11, 12 Oct 2025; every entrant gets a Competitive Success Index and a self-analysis report. => IMPLICATION: The addressable paid price band for a practice+test product is roughly ₹999–₹3,500/year. Anchor at ₹1,499–₹1,999/year with a free PYQ tier. TALLENTEX is the template for your rewards layer: a paid-entry (₹300) scholarship/contest exam that funds itself, produces a ranked cohort, generates a personalised report as the deliverable, and converts winners into paid users. That maps directly onto your coins/giveaways/contests plan and is a proven Indian mechanic.
- (high) Testbook's 'Pass One' shows where subscription packaging is heading: Testbook has merged Pass, Pass Pro, Pass Pro Max and Pass Elite into a single unified 'Pass One' subscription. Scope: 70,000+ mock tests and previous year papers across 700+ exams; full-length mocks, sectional tests, chapter tests; unlimited re-attempts on any mock or PYQ; All India Rank; 'Pro Live Tests' taken simultaneously with thousands of others. 'Smart Analysis' surfaces percentile and standing vs competitors, flags which questions consumed too much time, and breaks chapter-wise accuracy into strong / average / weak buckets. iOS 4.55★ / 22,169 ratings, v2.1.2 (14 Jul 2026), 63.9 MB — notably the leanest binary among major Indian prep apps. => IMPLICATION: Two lessons: (1) tier proliferation is being reversed industry-wide — ship ONE paid tier, not four; (2) 'Pro Live Tests' (synchronised, all-India, everyone sits the same paper at the same minute) is the highest-engagement format in the category and maps perfectly onto your server-authoritative timer + Redis leaderboard architecture. Schedule weekly all-India live mocks as the retention spine. Also note Testbook ships at 63.9 MB vs PW's 428.8 MB — bundle size is a competitive variable on Indian Android.
- (high) UWorld is the gold-standard QBank UX; here is the full feature and price spec to copy: USMLE Step 1 QBank: 3,600+ questions at or above exam difficulty. Pricing: 30-day $349; 90-day $459 (+1 Self-Assessment); 180-day $519 (+2 SAs, QBank reset, Study Planner, 1,000 UAsk prompts); 360-day $579 (+3 SAs); 730-day $749. Self-Assessments $50 each for 2-week access, 8 blocks × 20 questions, scored as a 3-digit score plus equated percentage correct; 3-form bundle $120. Biostatistics review $25/90 days. Features: ReadyDecks (2,000+ premade flashcards); SmartCards (custom spaced-repetition flashcards supporting images, graphs and tables, generated while reviewing); My Notebook (digital notes with advanced search); Study Planner (auto-schedules by declared availability); topic-level performance data; peer-to-peer percentile comparisons; custom quiz builder; Tutor Mode (explanation after each question) vs Timed Mode (exam simulation); custom blocks drawn from the bank; Medical Library of 1,200+ peer-reviewed articles integrated with the QBank; UAsk AI study assistant (100–1,000 prompts by tier); explanations built on "award-winning illustrations, labeled diagrams, tables, and charts"; iOS and Android apps with cross-device progress sync. Over 90% of US medical students use it for Step 1. => IMPLICATION: The transplantable primitives, in priority order: (1) Tutor Mode vs Timed Mode as a first-class toggle — nobody in India ships this; (2) per-option rationales, not just 'correct answer is B' — explain why each distractor is wrong; (3) a searchable persistent Notebook that survives across tests and links back to source questions; (4) SmartCards — let a student one-tap convert a wrong answer into a spaced-repetition card carrying the question image and your LaTeX; (5) custom block builder with filters (subject/chapter/topic/difficulty/PYQ year/unused/incorrect/marked); (6) 'unused vs incorrect vs marked' question state as a first-class filter — this is UWorld's most-imitated feature globally and is entirely absent from Indian apps; (7) QBank reset as a paid-tier perk. Note the price ladder shape: longer duration barely costs more, which drives annual commitment — mirror that with ₹1,999/yr vs ₹599/quarter.
- (high) Anki's FSRS is free, open source, default, and empirically better — there is no reason to build your own scheduler: FSRS (Free Spaced Repetition Scheduler), created by Jarrett Ye, has been available in Anki since v23.10 (Oct 2023) and is now the default scheduler. Trained on ~700 million real reviews from ~20,000 Anki users. Delivers roughly 20–30% fewer reviews than SM-2 for equivalent retention. Open-source implementations exist as libraries (e.g. the `fsrs` package on PyPI, plus Rust/TypeScript ports). AnkiHub, the commercial layer for community-maintained decks (most notably AnKing Step 1/Step 2), costs ~$6/month (raised once from $5) and syncs errata, new cards and guideline updates into a user's deck WITHOUT wiping review history; scholarships offered. Peer-reviewed 2024-2025 evidence supports spaced repetition: one paediatrics cohort scored 16.24 with spaced repetition vs 11.89 with traditional methods. => IMPLICATION: Port an FSRS implementation into the app (JS/TS port runs client-side in React Native; store review logs in Postgres for retraining). Auto-generate cards from: every incorrect answer, every question marked for review, every formula in a chapter. This is the single largest UX gap between Indian apps and the global gold standard, it is cheap to build, and it produces a daily-return habit that is pedagogically honest — unlike streak-only gamification. Critical detail from AnkiHub: content updates must never destroy a student's review history. Design your question versioning so that editing a question's text or fixing an error preserves per-student scheduling state.
- (high) Duolingo's retention numbers give you the design targets for the rewards layer: Users with 7+ day streaks retain at 2.4x the rate of users who never establish a streak. Streak Freeze (protects 1-2 days) reduced churn by 21% among users at risk of breaking a streak. Introducing Leagues increased lesson completion by 25%; users actively engaging with XP leaderboards complete 40% more lessons per week. Duolingo DAU reached ~47.7M in 2025, growing ~36-40% YoY; churn fell from 47% (2020) to ~28% in major markets. The published design lesson: mechanics that retain a user on day one differ from those that retain at month six, so gamification must be layered by tenure segment. => IMPLICATION: Concrete spec for your coins/streaks/leaderboards: (a) the streak must be earnable by a small, guaranteed-completable action (e.g. 10 questions or one flashcard review), not by finishing a 3-hour mock; (b) ship a streak-freeze equivalent — buy it with promotional coins, this is exactly what coins should be for, and it is the single highest-ROI mechanic; (c) leaderboards must be bucketed cohorts of ~30 peers with promotion/relegation, NOT a global all-India table where a rank-40,000 student sees nothing actionable — Redis sorted sets per bucket, rebalanced weekly; (d) do not tie coins to spending money; tie them to consistency so the reward loop is aligned with outcomes. Serious caveat: NEET/JEE aspirants are under documented psychological strain (12 suicides linked to the 2026 leak). Streak loss-aversion must be tuned down relative to Duolingo — offer forgiveness generously and never send guilt-framed notifications.
- (high) Free government competition: SATHEE (MoE + IIT Kanpur) is credible and zero-cost: SATHEE (Self-Assessment Test and Help for Entrance Exams), launched by the Ministry of Education with IIT Kanpur, covers JEE, NEET, SSC, CUET, CLAT, ICAR and RRB entirely free. Features: structured study plan, live and recorded sessions by IITians, daily practice problems, All India Mock Test Series plus chapter-wise tests, AI performance analytics that identify weak/strong areas and recommend targeted lectures and questions, 'Solve with Me' sessions, live doubt-clearing 10 AM–6 PM, and mentorship from IIT/AIIMS alumni. Quizzes, mock tests and NCERT notes available in 12 Indian languages. Ran a 40-day free JEE Main crash course from 1 Nov 2025 for the Jan 2026 session (satheejee.iitk.ac.in). Separately, NTA's own Abhyas app supplies official practice questions that MARKS and others surface. => IMPLICATION: You cannot win on 'free content' — the government gives away lectures, DPPs, mock tests and AI analytics in 12 languages. You win on execution quality, speed, mobile UX, and the specific loops (spaced repetition on your own errors, cohort leaderboards, tutor mode) that SATHEE does not build. Also: 12-language support is now a government-set baseline expectation; at minimum plan English + Hindi UI strings and language-tagged content from day one, because 'mixed Hindi/English content' is a top-5 complaint at Testbook.
- (high) iOS ratings snapshot, India storefront, July 2026 (raw comparables): Pulled live from the iTunes Search API for country=in: Physics Wallah 4.31★ / 14,432 ratings, v10.1, 428.81 MB, min iOS 14.1, updated 2026-07-20. ALLEN 4.16★ / 1,667, v4.11.2, 185.96 MB, min iOS 15.0, updated 2026-07-21. Unacademy Learner 4.49★ / 54,284, v2.3.4, 154.8 MB, updated 2025-07-10. Unacademy Educator 3.33★ / 391, v1.9.40, updated 2024-01-11. Testbook 4.55★ / 22,169, v2.1.2, 63.9 MB, updated 2026-07-14. Adda247 4.32★ / 9,886, v1.16.8, 155.2 MB, updated 2025-10-31. Vedantu Learning 3.93★ / 198, v2.8.1, 52.2 MB, updated 2026-04-09. EMBIBE 4.64★ / 2,093, v1.0.79, updated 2026-07-16. Aakash App for JEE & NEET 3.67★ / 6 ratings, v1.0.14, updated 2025-12-17. Utkarsh 3.60★ / 3,085, v8.0, updated 2026-06-24. Extramarks 4.17★ / 4,899, updated 2026-07-24. Xylem Education 4.25★ / 859. Global comparables in the same storefront: Khan Academy 4.75★ / 7,707; Brainly 4.48★ / 37,720; Photomath 4.64★ / 4,535. => IMPLICATION: Two exploitable facts. First, iOS rating volumes are tiny relative to the cohort (Vedantu has 198 iOS ratings; Aakash's new app has 6) — India is Android-dominant and iOS is under-served, so a polished iOS build is cheap share. Second, the entire category clusters at 3.6–4.6★ with the failure modes documented above; 4.7★+ is achievable purely on stability and honest sales, and Khan Academy at 4.75★ proves Indian users will rate a well-built free education app highly.
- (high) Embibe: strongest 'adaptive AI' claims in India, but content-quality and doubt-SLA complaints: EMBIBE: Learning Outcomes App, Indiavidual Learning Limited (Reliance-backed), 4.64★ / 2,093 iOS ratings, v1.0.79 (16 Jul 2026). Claims 400+ exams, multiple languages, interactive live classes, AI-powered coaching, adaptive practice, 24/7 doubt resolution, immersive 3D videos, customised learning paths; subscriptions ₹709–₹899/month with 7-day free trials. Satellite apps: Embibe Classroom (teacher-side — homework assignment, attendance, leave approvals, per-student strength/weakness analytics, adaptive practice analysis, cumulative reports, customisable assignments with progress filters; 5.0★ / 9 ratings), Embibe Homework (student companion, 5.0★ / 2 ratings), EMBIBE Lens (AR textbook scanning to 3D models with labels, videos, full solutions, simulations; 4.82★ / 72), Embibe Lab Experiments (500+ virtual CBSE/NCERT experiments; 4.89★ / 54). Complaints: 'Mistakes in questions' (Richa 1983, 31/07/2023) — "I have problem with mistakes in questions asked and some times there is no option to write the answer"; spelling-sensitive auto-grading marks correct answers wrong; 'Embibe for doubts' (ANJALI_NAIRrrrr, 29/11/2023) — "I have been trying to ask a math doubt for a few days now but have not gotten any response." => IMPLICATION: ₹709–899/month (₹8,500–10,800/yr) is the ceiling of the market and Embibe's tiny rating counts on satellite apps suggest weak adoption at that price. More useful: their failure modes are your QA spec. Numeric/integer answer grading must be tolerance-based and normalisation-aware (not string equality), every question needs an in-app 'report an error' button routed to an ADMIN moderation queue with SLA, and doubt requests need a visible state machine with an explicit SLA timer — an unanswered doubt with no status is the most common trust-killer in the category.
- (high) Exam formats you must model exactly in the test engine: JEE Main 2026 Paper 1 (B.E./B.Tech): 75 questions total, 25 each in Physics, Chemistry, Mathematics; Section A = 20 MCQs, Section B = 5 numerical-value questions per subject with NO optional selection — the COVID-era 'attempt 5 of 10' option was scrapped from JEE Main 2025 onward per NTA notification citing WHO's 5 May 2023 end of the public health emergency. All 5 Section B questions compulsory. Duration 180 minutes, 300 marks. NEET UG 2026: 180 compulsory MCQs — Physics 45 (180 marks), Chemistry 45 (180 marks), Biology 90 (360 marks) = 720 marks; +4 correct, -1 incorrect; standard duration 180 minutes, though the 21 June 2026 re-exam ran 195 minutes. NEET's optional Section B was also removed. JEE Advanced remains the outlier: multi-format (single-correct, multiple-correct with partial marking, integer, matching-list, paragraph) with varying and sometimes negative partial-credit schemes that change year to year. => IMPLICATION: Your marking engine cannot be a single boolean scorer. Model marking as a per-question-type strategy object versioned by exam and year: MCQ_SINGLE(+4/-1), NUMERIC(+4/0, tolerance-based), MULTI_CORRECT_PARTIAL (JEE Adv: +4 all correct, +3/+2/+1 partial, -2 any wrong), MATCH_LIST, INTEGER. Store the scheme with the paper, not in code, so ADMINs can author a 2027 pattern change without a release. Sectioned papers need per-section time locks and a section-switch confirmation, mirroring NTA. Given NEET goes CBT in 2027, ship the NTA palette (Not Visited / Not Answered / Answered / Marked for Review / Answered & Marked) with exactly NTA's colours and semantics.
- (high) Vedantu and Aakash are structurally weak — the mid-market is vacating: Vedantu FY25: revenue ₹227 Cr (+23% from ₹185 Cr), loss before tax ₹210 Cr (+25% from ₹168.5 Cr); online tutoring = 87% of operating revenue at ₹197 Cr (+19%). iOS app only 198 ratings at 3.93★. Aakash Educational Services: FY24 revenue ₹2,471.14 Cr (+1% YoY); Byju's lost control of Aakash after failing acquisition terms and wrote off its full ~$1B investment; Byju's entered insolvency proceedings in 2024 and exited the Play Store. Aakash's new standalone 'Aakash App for JEE & NEET' (v1.0.14, Dec 2025) has just 6 iOS ratings at 3.67★ — effectively no traction. Toppr, a Byju's subsidiary, saw 1,500+ layoffs and has had no visible product investment; it survives only as a legacy brand in listicles. Doubtnut sold to Allen for ~$10M in 2023 after rejecting ~$150M from Byju's. => IMPLICATION: There is no credible mid-market incumbent left between PW's ₹2,000-5,000 batches and Allen's premium offline. Brand-name gravity is weak enough that a well-executed independent product can take practice/test share without needing star faculty. But note the corollary: video-lecture businesses are capital-intensive and dying; a practice-and-analytics business with YouTube solution links (your stated model) has far better unit economics and is the correct structural bet.
- (high) 2025-2026 entrant wave is uniformly AI-tutor-shaped and uniformly early: Tutoz — IIT Palakkad, launched Aug 2025 by Education Minister Dharmendra Pradhan; adaptive study plans, smart analytics, mock testing, AI mentoring for JEE/NEET/CAT. YoLearn.ai — launched Jan 2025 by Kirti Prakash Mishra, raised ~$500K (₹4.3 Cr) pre-seed, voice-first real-time AI tutor. Lytmus AI — Bengaluru, ₹5 Cr pre-seed led by Boundless Ventures (June 2026), AI mentorship starting with NEET. ProLearn — ex-Vedantu director Ravneet Singh, ₹30 Cr pre-seed from BEENEXT (June 2026), AI-native K-12 companion for JEE/NEET. PrepNexus — 24/7 AI doubt solving for JEE/NEET. Careers360 launched an AI-powered JEE Main and NEET prep app. Also visible in the App Store India catalogue as new small entrants: ExamBro – JEE NEET Paper Maker (4.85★ / 196, v1.2.6, Jun 2026 — teacher-side paper generation), ExoPrep: PYQ NEET JEE Prep (v1.0, May 2026, 'real PYQs, timed mock tests, analytics, offline flashcards'), Logic Bloom (gamified NCERT, AI mentorship, competitive dueling), iScholar, ExamBattle, CW JEE/NEET (2.88★ / 8). => IMPLICATION: Everyone is building an AI tutor; nobody is building a better question bank and test engine. AI doubt-solving is becoming a commodity feature (PW's Saarthi, Allen's AI Doubt Support, Embibe, SATHEE, and six startups all ship it) — do not lead with it, but you cannot omit it. The genuinely underbuilt layer is disciplined content operations: correctly tagged, error-free, PYQ-provenanced questions with per-option rationales. Note ExamBro at 4.85★ specifically serving teachers generating papers — validation that ADMIN-side authoring tooling has real, unserved demand.
- (high) Doubt-solving SLA is the most reliably broken promise in the category: Filo differentiates purely on this: upload a photo or type a question, connect to a live tutor in under 60 seconds via video/audio, 24×7 — winner of a Google Play Best Of Users' Choice Award and 'Best Education Startup of the Year'; premium pricing has reportedly risen over time (unverified). Against that, the complaint record: Allen — "slow doubt resolution"; PW — Saarthi unavailable on iPad for paid users, doubt submission failing with 'dRoomId is null or undefined', and repeated requests to restore the 24-hour doubt facility; Embibe — a maths doubt unanswered for days; Unacademy — "no response after 4 days despite promised 10-12 hour reply time" and no reply after 8-10 days of emails; Testbook — "No refund. No response." ALLEN Digital ships 'AI Doubt Support' and SATHEE offers live doubt-clearing only 10 AM–6 PM. => IMPLICATION: If you offer doubts at all, offer a promise you can keep. A safer design: (1) surface the existing solution + YouTube link first, (2) then peer/community answers, (3) then AI, (4) then human escalation with an explicit visible countdown and an auto-refund of coins if the SLA is breached. The state machine and the countdown matter more than the answer speed — every complaint above is about silence, not slowness.
- (high) Offline support is claimed everywhere and works nowhere: Documented offline/download failures: PW iPad users report lecture downloads "very slow even on 5G"; Allen's PDF viewer lags, cannot zoom or highlight properly, and does not remember reading position; Allen users report study materials (notes, lectures, practice questions) becoming entirely inaccessible for some subjects. The only apps that reliably work offline in this catalogue are static-content ones — 'Ncert Books & Solutions' (4.62★ / 24,807 ratings) explicitly sells 'offline NCERT textbooks, solutions, previous year papers' with bookmarking and highlighting. MARKS's differentiator is a lightweight, distraction-free experience rather than heavy downloads. New entrant ExoPrep lists 'offline flashcards' as a headline feature. => IMPLICATION: Practice content is small — a chapter's worth of LaTeX questions plus compressed images is a few MB versus a 500 MB video. Ship genuine offline practice: pre-download question sets, answer offline, queue attempts, sync on reconnect with conflict resolution. This is easy for you (Expo + SQLite/MMKV + a Supabase sync queue) and impossible for video-first incumbents. Critical constraint: an offline-capable client must NEVER hold the mock-test timer or the answer key — keep the timer server-authoritative and only allow offline mode for untimed practice, or you will have both cheating and a scoring dispute problem.
- (high) Teacher/admin-side tooling is near-universally abandoned: Unacademy Educator App: 3.33★ / 391 ratings, v1.9.40, last updated 11 Jan 2024 — 2.5 years stale while the learner app has 54,284 ratings. Embibe Classroom (the most feature-complete teacher tool found: homework assignment, attendance, leave approvals, per-student strength/weakness analytics, adaptive practice analysis, cumulative reporting, customisable assignments, progress filtering) has 9 total iOS ratings. Embibe Homework has 2. Allen exposes no public teacher app. Vedantu Partner launched Jul 2026 with 0 ratings. The only well-rated teacher-facing product in the India App Store catalogue is ExamBro – JEE NEET Paper Maker at 4.85★ / 196 ratings (v1.2.6, Jun 2026) — a standalone paper-generation utility, not part of any major platform. => IMPLICATION: Your ADMIN role is a differentiator, not overhead. Build it as a real product: LaTeX-first authoring with live KaTeX preview, image-question upload with crop/OCR-assisted tagging, bulk CSV/JSON import, duplicate detection against the existing bank, a review/approval workflow (draft → reviewed → published), version history that preserves student attempt/scheduling state, blueprint-driven paper generation (specify chapter weightage, difficulty mix, PYQ-year mix and auto-assemble), and an error-report triage queue. This is what converts individual coaching centres and small institutes into paying customers, and no incumbent is defending that ground.
- (medium) LaTeX/math rendering and image-based questions are an unglamorous but decisive technical risk: Evidence of the failure surface: Allen's most-cited complaint is a laggy PDF viewer that cannot zoom or highlight and loses reading position — the fallback everyone uses when they cannot render math natively. Embibe's reported defects are answer-input related: "some times there is no option to write the answer" and spelling-sensitive grading marking correct answers wrong. Aakash and Allen still ship a meaningful share of their test series as pen-and-paper (Allen NEET: 11 CBT + 8 pen-and-paper of 19 tests), implying the digital rendering path is not trusted for full-fidelity papers. Meanwhile MARKS, whose entire product is rendered PYQs at 4.8★ across 40,000+ Play reviews with 2M+ questions solved daily, demonstrates that native question rendering at scale is solvable. => IMPLICATION: Do not use PDFs as the delivery format for questions — that single decision is the root cause of the category's worst UX complaints. Render LaTeX natively (KaTeX in a hardened WebView or a native math renderer), pre-measure and cache layout, and support pinch-zoom on question images with a full-screen viewer. For numeric input, build a purpose-made keypad (digits, decimal, minus, exponent) rather than the OS keyboard, grade with configurable absolute/relative tolerance, and never string-compare. Store both a LaTeX source and a rendered-image fallback per question so that a rendering failure degrades to a picture rather than to a blank.
- (high) (a) Table-stakes feature set a student expects in 2026: Non-negotiable, present in nearly every serious competitor: (1) free, complete, chapter-and-topic-tagged PYQ bank for JEE Main, JEE Advanced and NEET with year tags — MARKS sets this at 1 lakh+ questions, free; (2) custom test builder filtered by subject/chapter/topic/difficulty; (3) full-length mocks in the exact NTA CBT interface with the standard question palette; (4) correct per-exam marking schemes including JEE Advanced partial credit; (5) All India Rank / percentile after every mock; (6) chapter-wise accuracy split into strong/average/weak; (7) time-spent-per-question with an overtime flag (Quizrr, Testbook); (8) text AND video solutions — YouTube linking is accepted and normal; (9) some form of AI doubt support (PW Saarthi, Allen AI Doubt Support, Embibe, SATHEE, six startups); (10) bookmarks/saved questions and a notes facility; (11) daily practice targets, a streak, and a leaderboard (MARKS, Duolingo-derived); (12) formula sheets / revision cards / mind maps (MARKS Formula Cards, PW Utsav kit, Oswaal360); (13) Hindi + English content and UI (SATHEE ships 12 languages; language mixing is a top complaint at Testbook); (14) offline access to at least notes/PDFs; (15) unlimited re-attempts of any mock (Testbook Pass One). => IMPLICATION: Budget your v1 to fully cover 1-8 and 10-11 before shipping anything novel. Any of these missing at launch reads as 'incomplete' and will show up in your first 1★ reviews. Items 9, 12, 13, 14 can be v1.1. Do NOT ship a paid tier until 1-8 are solid and free.
- (high) (b) Where the market is genuinely underserved: Ranked by (unmet demand × ease of building on your stack): (1) Spaced repetition on your own errors — no significant Indian JEE/NEET app ships an FSRS-grade scheduler, despite Anki's algorithm being free, open source, and empirically 20-30% more efficient than SM-2; Allen's 'Improvement Book' and 'Flash Card' are the closest and are static. (2) Tutor Mode vs Timed Mode — UWorld's core distinction, absent in India. (3) Question-state filters (unused / incorrect / marked / correct-but-guessed) as a first-class way to build a test — absent. (4) Per-option rationales (why each distractor is wrong) — Indian solutions are near-universally 'the answer with working', not distractor analysis. (5) A persistent searchable notebook that links back to source questions — Quizrr has a notebook section, nobody has search+backlinks. (6) Honest self-serve commerce with a real refund policy and no telesales — a brand position, not a feature. (7) Cohort-sized leaderboards (~30 peers, promotion/relegation) instead of futile all-India tables. (8) Real offline practice with attempt sync. (9) iOS quality — MARKS has no native iOS app at all; category iOS rating counts are tiny. (10) Teacher/admin authoring — Unacademy's educator app is 2.5 years stale, Embibe Classroom has 9 ratings, and standalone ExamBro at 4.85★ proves the demand. (11) Confidence-tagged answering (mark 'sure / unsure / guessed' before submitting) — used in medical prep abroad, absent here, and it is the cheapest possible input to a genuinely useful analytics layer. => IMPLICATION: Your differentiation stack, in build order: FSRS-driven error review → question-state filters + tutor/timed modes → per-option rationales → cohort leaderboards + streak-freeze coins → offline practice → admin authoring suite. Items 1-4 are all engine-level and compound with each other; ship them together as 'the practice engine' rather than as separate features.
- (high) (c) UWorld/Anki UX patterns missing from Indian apps: Missing from essentially all of PW, Allen, Aakash, Unacademy, Vedantu, Embibe, Testbook, Adda247, eSaral, Utkarsh: (i) Tutor Mode (immediate explanation per question) vs Timed Mode toggle; (ii) explanation of every distractor, with labelled diagrams — UWorld explicitly markets "award-winning illustrations, labeled diagrams, tables, and charts" as the product; (iii) SmartCards — one-tap conversion of a missed question into a spaced-repetition card carrying its image/graph/table; (iv) ReadyDecks equivalent — a curated premade card deck (UWorld ships 2,000+); (v) My Notebook with advanced search across all notes; (vi) Study Planner that auto-schedules from declared daily availability and syllabus deadlines; (vii) QBank reset as a discrete, valued entitlement; (viii) topic-level peer percentile (not just overall rank); (ix) self-assessment exams sold and scored separately with a predicted-score output (UWorld: $50, 8 blocks × 20 questions, 3-digit score + equated percentage correct); (x) content updates that preserve review history — AnkiHub's core promise; (xi) FSRS-quality scheduling with per-user parameter optimisation trained on the user's own review log. => IMPLICATION: (i), (ii), (iii), (v) and (viii) are all achievable inside your existing Supabase + Redis design with no new infrastructure. (vi) Study Planner and (ix) scored self-assessments with predicted rank are the two highest-perceived-value paid features — a 'predicted JEE Main percentile / NEET rank' after each full mock, computed from your own cohort distribution, is exactly what Quizrr charges for and what students screenshot and share. (x) is a schema decision you must make now: question edits must not invalidate per-student SRS state.
- (high) (d) What students actually complain about most, ranked by frequency across 1-2★ reviews: From verbatim App Store reviews across Allen, PW, Unacademy, Testbook and Embibe (Jul 2023 – Jul 2026): 1. Crashes and broken login/session — "crashes constantly...login system is broken" (Allen 1★); repeated 'duplicate connection' errors on a single device; login crashes. 2. Aggressive telesales + no refund — "10-20 calls weekly for purchases but impossible to reach customer support" (Unacademy, Feb 2025); "money is non refundable and non cancellation policy, guyz its a scam" (Testbook, Jan 2026); "they will call you again and again to buy the subscription and apply a mandate". 3. Support silence — "no response after 4 days despite promised 10-12 hour reply time"; "no response to emails after 8-10 days"; a maths doubt unanswered for days (Embibe). 4. Missing or unuploaded content after payment — "half lectures missing"; "course not accessible with exam 2 months away"; lectures not uploaded with no in-app notice. 5. PDF/document viewer quality — lags, cannot zoom or highlight, does not remember position (Allen). 6. Platform/tablet parity — no iPad landscape (Allen, multiple); Community tab, Saarthi and Infinite Mentorship missing on iPad for paying PW users. 7. Streaming/playback defects — lectures stopping on full internet; recordings sticking above 1.25x; downloads slow on 5G; requests for stable 1080p. 8. Content errors and grading bugs — "mistakes in questions asked"; spelling-sensitive grading; "no option to write the answer"; practice questions whose stated difficulty does not match reality. 9. Language mixing — "60% in English and 40% in Hindi, which I can't understand". 10. Timing UX — poll timers expiring before the student can answer. => IMPLICATION: Seven of the top ten are engineering-quality or operations issues you control completely, and two (telesales, refunds) are business-model choices you can simply decline to make. Turn this list into an explicit pre-launch QA checklist and a public 'what we promise' page: no sales calls, published refund window, published doubt SLA, status banner for any content still being uploaded, and a visible 'report an error on this question' affordance. This complaint list, addressed literally, is a 4.7★+ app.
RECOMMENDATIONS:
- Build the practice engine, not another content platform. The video-lecture business is capital-intensive and structurally impaired (Vedantu ₹227 Cr revenue against a ₹210 Cr loss; Unacademy sold at <$500M vs a $3.5B peak; Byju's insolvent). Your stated model — question bank + test engine + YouTube solution links — has an order-of-magnitude better cost structure. Position explicitly against the incumbents' own admission: Unacademy's CEO said in March 2026 that the sector "has not seen enough real product innovation in recent years."
- Ship a pixel-faithful NTA CBT simulator as the flagship, and market it against the 2027 NEET CBT transition. Reproduce the exact question palette semantics and colours (Not Visited / Not Answered / Answered / Marked for Review / Answered and Marked for Review), per-section timers and section locks, Save & Next / Clear Response / Mark for Review & Next controls, and the submit-confirmation summary. NEET moves to CBT from 2027 per the Education Minister, so ~23 lakh NEET aspirants will be practising for an interface they have never sat. Keep the timer server-authoritative (Postgres server_started_at + Edge Function heartbeat; never trust device clock) and treat the sectioned-paper model as a first-class schema object.
- Port FSRS (open source, Anki's default since v23.10, trained on ~700M reviews, 20-30% fewer reviews than SM-2) and drive it from the student's own errors. Auto-generate review cards from every incorrect answer, every marked-for-review question, and every formula. Run the scheduler client-side in React Native, persist review logs to Postgres, and optimise per-user parameters from those logs. Design the question schema so that editing or correcting a question preserves each student's scheduling state — that non-destructive-update guarantee is AnkiHub's core value proposition and no Indian app offers it.
- Copy UWorld's engine primitives verbatim: a Tutor Mode / Timed Mode toggle; question-state filters (unused / incorrect / correct / marked / guessed) in the custom-test builder alongside subject, chapter, topic, difficulty, exam tag and PYQ year; per-option rationales explaining why each distractor is wrong, not just the correct working; a persistent Notebook with full-text search and backlinks to source questions; topic-level peer percentile; and a paid 'QBank reset'. Mirror UWorld's price-ladder shape (30d $349 vs 730d $749 — long durations barely cost more) to push annual commitment: e.g. free PYQ tier, ₹599/quarter, ₹1,799/year.
- Make analytics actionable rather than decorative, benchmarked against Quizrr's 15-page report. Per mock, show: predicted percentile/rank from your own cohort distribution; time-per-question versus cohort median with an overtime flag; a mistake taxonomy the student self-tags at review time (conceptual / calculation / misread / silly / guessed-right / unattempted); chapter weightage-versus-your-accuracy as a 2D scatter that names the next three chapters to study; and an accuracy-versus-confidence matrix if you implement pre-submit confidence tagging. Ship the report as a shareable image — screenshotting and sharing rank cards is the category's organic growth loop.
- Adopt a no-telesales, transparent-refund business model and market it as a feature. The most frequent 1-2★ complaints across Unacademy and Testbook are spam calls, auto-mandates and refusal to refund. Sell entirely self-serve, publish a refund window on the pricing screen, never make a sales call, and if you must collect a phone number for OTP auth, state in-app that it will never be used for sales. This costs nothing to build and is a durable brand position no funded incumbent can copy without breaking its own revenue model.
- Design the rewards layer on Duolingo's proven primitives but tuned for exam-stress sensitivity. Make the streak earnable by a small guaranteed action (10 questions or one flashcard session, not a 3-hour mock); sell a streak-freeze with promotional coins (Duolingo's streak freeze cut at-risk churn 21%); use bucketed ~30-peer leaderboards with weekly promotion and relegation via Redis sorted sets rather than a futile all-India table (leagues lifted lesson completion 25%); and tie coins to consistency, never to spending. Given the documented psychological toll on this cohort (12 suicides linked to the 2026 NEET leak), forgive streak breaks generously and never send guilt-framed notifications.
- Treat the ADMIN role as a shippable product, because it is the unclaimed B2B2C wedge. Unacademy's educator app is 3.33★ and 2.5 years without an update; Embibe Classroom has 9 iOS ratings; ExamBro, a standalone teacher paper-generation utility, sits at 4.85★ / 196 ratings. Build: LaTeX-first authoring with live KaTeX preview, image-question upload with crop and OCR-assisted tagging, bulk import with duplicate detection against the existing bank, draft → review → publish workflow, immutable version history, blueprint-driven paper assembly (specify chapter weightage, difficulty mix and PYQ-year mix, auto-generate), and a triage queue fed by student error reports. This is what sells to coaching centres.
- Engineer against the category's specific documented failure modes rather than generic quality goals. Concretely: never deliver questions as PDFs (Allen's laggy, non-zoomable, position-losing PDF viewer is its single most-cited defect); render LaTeX natively with a rendered-image fallback per question; build a custom numeric keypad and grade numerics with configurable tolerance, never string equality (Embibe marks correct answers wrong on spelling); support landscape and tablet from day one (repeated iPad complaints at Allen and PW); make auth survive token refresh and multiple devices without a hard 'duplicate connection' eviction; keep the binary small (Testbook 63.9 MB versus PW 428.8 MB); and never render a silent empty state — every failure needs a typed message and a retry.
- Ship genuine offline practice, which video-first incumbents structurally cannot. Pre-download question sets (a chapter of LaTeX plus compressed images is a few MB), allow untimed practice offline, queue attempts locally in SQLite/MMKV, and sync to Supabase on reconnect with explicit conflict resolution. Hard constraint: never let the offline client hold the timer or the answer key for a graded mock — restrict offline to untimed practice and flashcard review, and keep all scored mock sessions server-authoritative.
- Price the free tier at MARKS parity or better and monetise the loops MARKS cannot. MARKS is free forever with 1 lakh+ PYQs, custom tests, formula cards, daily challenges and leaderboards at 1M+ downloads and 4.8★ — so a complete free PYQ bank plus a custom test builder plus the CBT engine is your cost of entry, not your product. Monetise: FSRS error review, full-length sectioned mocks with predicted rank, video/rich-text solutions, tutor mode, and the notebook. Exploit MARKS's structural gap by shipping a first-class native iOS app — MARKS serves iOS only as a web app and the whole category's iOS rating counts are small (Vedantu 198, Aakash 6).
PITFALLS:
- Do not treat 10,000 concurrent as a steady-state number. Load in this market is violently spiky — it clusters on scheduled all-India mock windows, result days, and the Jan/Apr/May exam months. Supabase connection pooling (PgBouncer/Supavisor) and RLS-heavy queries are the likely bottleneck: every RLS policy is a per-row predicate, and a naive 'students can read their own attempts' policy over a large attempts table will degrade badly under a synchronised 10,000-student submit. Precompute leaderboards and rank in Redis, write attempts through a queue rather than direct client inserts, and load-test the exact minute when 10,000 papers auto-submit simultaneously. KCET's 2026 results site crashed under exactly this pattern.
- Never let the client own the mock timer or hold the answer key. Randomised question order per student plus client-side timing is a cheating surface: students will inspect network payloads, and any pre-shipped answer key or solution blob will be extracted and circulated. Given the 2026 NEET leak (120-140 of 180 questions matched a leaked paper, ₹2-10 lakh paid per access, CBI investigation, and a pending amendment raising sentences beyond 10 years), content leakage is now a legal and reputational hazard, not just a business one. Serve questions one screen at a time or in small signed batches, withhold correct answers until server-side submission, watermark rendered images per user session, and never expose a bulk question-export endpoint to STUDENT-role tokens.
- Do not build 'AI doubt solving' as your headline differentiator. It is already commoditised — PW Saarthi, Allen AI Doubt Support, Embibe, SATHEE, Tutoz, YoLearn.ai, Lytmus AI, ProLearn and PrepNexus all ship it, and the free government platform includes AI performance analytics in 12 languages. Worse, doubt-solving is the single most-broken promise in the category's review record (Unacademy 8-10 days of silence, Embibe days for one maths doubt, PW's Saarthi missing on iPad for paying users). If you promise an SLA you cannot keep, you inherit the category's worst complaint. Make the escalation ladder explicit and visible, with an auto-refund of coins on SLA breach.
- Do not underestimate content operations. Every serious competitor gets caught on question errors, mis-tagged difficulty, wrong answer keys and grading bugs — Embibe on spelling-sensitive grading and 'mistakes in questions', Allen on practice-question difficulty not matching its stated level. A 1 lakh-question bank with a 1% error rate is 1,000 wrong questions, and each one that a student encounters during a scored mock destroys trust irrecoverably. Budget for a reviewer workflow, a mandatory second-approver step before publish, an in-app 'report an error' button on every question, and a moderation queue with an SLA — before you scale the bank.
- Do not copy Duolingo's loss-aversion intensity into an exam-stress context. Aggressive streak-break mechanics, public shaming, and guilt-framed push notifications are irresponsible for a cohort with documented suicide risk (at least 12 deaths linked to the 2026 NEET leak alone; NEET-related student suicide is an established subject of published media-analysis research). Ship streak freezes generously, keep leaderboards to small anonymous-ish peer cohorts rather than global all-India rankings, allow students to opt out of competitive surfaces entirely, and never send notifications framed as failure.
- Do not build four subscription tiers. Testbook has actively collapsed Pass, Pass Pro, Pass Pro Max and Pass Elite into a single 'Pass One', and the whole market is de-tiering. Multiple tiers create support load, entitlement bugs, and the exact 'I paid and this feature isn't available' complaint that recurs across PW (iPad missing paid features) and Unacademy (half lectures missing).
- Do not assume iOS parity comes free with Expo. Landscape and tablet layout failures are the single most repeated platform complaint in this category (Allen: broken on iPad Gen 11 in both orientations after a rotation; PW: paid features simply absent on iPad). React Native gives you code sharing, not layout correctness. Test explicitly on iPad and on low-RAM Android (the median Indian device), and keep the bundle small — PW ships 428.8 MB against Testbook's 63.9 MB.
- Do not assume the pricing data in this dossier is precise. Several vendors' pricing pages are client-rendered and return nothing to fetchers (allen.in test-series pages, quizrr.in FAQ answers), so the ALLEN ₹999 figure and Quizrr's price come from third-party or indirect sources and should be re-verified manually in-app before being used in any competitive pricing model.
OPEN QUESTIONS:
- Exact current INR pricing for Quizrr, ALLEN JEE/NEET online test series, and Allen Digital subscriptions — all three vendors render pricing client-side and return nothing to a fetcher. These need manual in-app or logged-in verification before use in a pricing model.
- Google Play ratings, review counts and install figures for the Indian apps (PW, Allen, Marks, Testbook, Unacademy, Vedantu, Adda247, Utkarsh, Oswaal360, Sarthaks). Play Store pages are JS-rendered and were not fetchable; all rating data in this dossier is iOS-only, which under-samples a market that is ~95% Android. A Play-scraper run or a SensorTower/AppFollow query is needed.
- Whether MARKS (getmarks.app) monetises at all, and how it funds 2M+ questions solved daily at zero revenue. If it is VC-funded and about to introduce a paid tier, the free-tier price floor could shift materially.
- Bulk 1-2★ Google Play review text at volume. This dossier's verbatim complaints are from Apple App Store India, which skews to a wealthier, smaller user base. Play reviews for the same apps likely surface different and more severe complaints (payment failures, low-RAM crashes, regional-language issues).
- Exact NTA CBT interface specification for the 2027 NEET transition — palette colours, control labels, section-lock behaviour, and whether an on-screen calculator or scratchpad is provided. NTA's official mock interface and the Abhyas app are the authoritative reference and should be captured screen by screen.
- Whether Doubtnut still operates as a distinct product under Allen in 2026, and what its current DAU and content assets are — it is absent from the Apple App Store India catalogue, suggesting it may have been folded into Allen Digital.
- Actual conversion and retention benchmarks for Indian test-prep apps (free-to-paid conversion rate, D1/D7/D30 retention, ARPU). PW's IPO prospectus (DRHP/RHP, Nov 2025) is the only public source likely to contain audited paid-user cohort data and should be read directly.
- Legal and content-licensing exposure for reproducing PYQs. NTA papers are public but coaching-institute solutions are not, and Allen/Aakash have historically pursued copyright claims. The safe path (original solutions plus links to third-party YouTube videos rather than rehosting) should be validated with counsel.
- Whether Embibe's ₹709-899/month pricing reflects real paying volume or is a list price with near-zero conversion — its satellite apps have single-digit rating counts, which suggests very low adoption.
- How MathonGo and Quizrr split economics and content, and whether their ~900-test bank is licensable — a content-partnership path could compress your cold-start timeline substantially.

## exam-fidelity
SUMMARY: Verified against primary PDFs (NTA JEE Main 2026 Information Bulletin, NTA NEET UG 2026 Information Bulletin, and the actual JEE Advanced 2026 Paper 1 & Paper 2 question papers from jeeadv.ac.in), not aggregator snippets. JEE Main 2026 Paper 1: 3 subjects x (Section A = 20 MCQ + Section B = 5 numerical), 75 questions, 300 marks, 180 min; Section B optional-choice is GONE (removed 2025) and Section B now carries -1 negative marking; NVQ answers must be "rounded off to the nearest integer". JEE Advanced 2026 (IIT Roorkee, 17 May 2026) is asymmetric: Paper 1 = 48 Q / 180 marks (per subject: 4 single-correct +3/0/-1; 4 multi-correct +4 with +3/+2/+1 partial and -1; 4 numerical to TWO decimals +4/0 with NO negative; 4 Matching List +4/0/-1), Paper 2 = 54 Q / 180 marks (4 single-correct; 5 multi-correct; 5 numerical; 2 question-stems x 2 numerical sub-questions at +2 each). Critically, the multi-correct penalty changed from -2 (2025 and earlier) to -1 in 2026, and Paper 1 dropped paragraph-type entirely — the pattern is genuinely not fixed year to year, so the marking rules MUST be data-driven per test, never hardcoded. NEET UG 2026 has no Section A/B at all: 180 compulsory questions, 720 marks, 180 minutes (14:00-17:00), +4/-1/0, pen-and-paper OMR, 13 languages; NEET 2026 was cancelled (3 May) and re-run on 21 June 2026, and NEET moves to CBT from 2027. Calculators are prohibited in JEE Main (NTA Public Notice 02 Nov 2025 called the Appendix-VIII on-screen-calculator line a typographic error) and in JEE Advanced — only an on-screen virtual numeric keypad exists. The NTA CBT console spec is fully documented in Appendix-VIII of the JEE Main bulletin: server-set countdown timer, 5-state question palette, Save & Next / Mark for Review & Next / Clear Response, palette-click navigation that does NOT save, free section switching, auto-advance on last question of a section, disabled physical keyboard, dark/light mode, font scaling and screen magnifier, and auto-submit at zero. Normalisation is percentile-per-shift on the TOTAL raw score to 7 decimal places (never an average of subject percentiles), with distinct tie-break chains for JEE Main (Maths→Phy→Chem→incorrect:correct ratios→same rank), JEE Advanced (positive marks→Maths→Physics→same rank) and NEET (Bio→Chem→Phy→incorrect:correct ratios→random by expert committee; age criterion abolished 2023).
FINDINGS:
- (high) JEE Main 2026 Paper 1 structure is fixed at 75 Q / 300 marks with NO optional questions in Section B: Official Information Bulletin JEE (Main) 2026, section 2.4: per subject Section A = 20 MCQs, Section B = 05 numerical-value questions, 100 marks each for Mathematics, Physics, Chemistry. Total = 75 questions, 300 marks. Verbatim: 'There will be negative marking for incorrect answer in Section A and Section B. For each question in Section B, a candidate has to enter the correct integer value of the answer using the mouse and the on-screen virtual numeric keypad... For Section B, the answer should be rounded off to the nearest integer.' The COVID-era 10-questions-attempt-any-5 choice was scrapped from JEE Main 2025 and is absent in the 2026 bulletin. Marking: MCQ +4 / -1 / 0 (unanswered or Marked for Review without selecting an option); NVQ +4 / -1 / 0 (unanswered or Marked for Review without filling a numerical value). Duration 03 hours (180 min); 04 hours for PwD/PwBD eligible for a scribe. Shifts 09:00-12:00 and 15:00-18:00 IST. Paper 2A/2B = 210 min. => IMPLICATION: Our JEE Main blueprint generator must emit exactly 3 sections x (20 MCQ + 5 NVQ), all 25 compulsory, with per-question-type marking rows stored in DB (not constants). NVQ answer storage must be an INTEGER with an equality check after rounding-to-nearest-integer of the student's entry — do NOT allow decimals or tolerance ranges for JEE Main (unlike JEE Advanced).
- (high) JEE Advanced 2026 Paper 1 exact section spec (parsed from the real paper, not a secondary summary): jeeadv.ac.in/documents/p1_english.pdf, per subject (Mathematics, Physics, Chemistry — identical structure, 60 marks each, 180 total, 48 questions): SECTION 1 (Max 12): FOUR questions, four options, ONLY ONE correct. Full +3 / Zero 0 if unanswered / Negative -1 in all other cases. SECTION 2 (Max 16): FOUR questions, ONE OR MORE THAN ONE correct. Full +4 only if all correct chosen; Partial +3 if all four options are correct but ONLY three chosen; Partial +2 if three or more options are correct but ONLY two chosen, both correct; Partial +1 if two or more correct but ONLY one chosen and it is correct; Zero 0 if unanswered; Negative -1 in all other cases. SECTION 3 (Max 16): FOUR questions, NUMERICAL VALUE, entered via mouse + on-screen virtual numeric keypad, 'If the numerical value has more than two decimal places, truncate/round-off the value to TWO decimal places'. Full +4 / Zero 0 in all other cases — NO negative marking. SECTION 4 (Max 16): FOUR Matching List Sets; List-I has FOUR entries (P)(Q)(R)(S), List-II has FIVE entries (1)-(5); four MCQ options, ONLY ONE satisfies. Full +4 / Zero 0 if unanswered / Negative -1 in all other cases. NOTE: Paper 1 2026 contained NO paragraph/comprehension section. => IMPLICATION: Four distinct scoring engines are required for Advanced Paper 1 alone: SINGLE_CORRECT(+3/-1), MULTI_CORRECT(partial ladder), NUMERICAL_2DP(no negative), MATCHING_LIST(+4/-1). Matching List needs a dedicated authoring UI: 4 left entries, 5 right entries, and 4 candidate permutation options — it is NOT a generic MCQ and needs its own render component.
- (high) JEE Advanced 2026 Paper 2 is structurally different from Paper 1 — asymmetric papers: jeeadv.ac.in/documents/p2_english.pdf, per subject (60 marks, 18 questions; 54 questions / 180 marks total): SECTION 1 (Max 12) FOUR single-correct, +3/0/-1. SECTION 2 (Max 20) FIVE multi-correct, +4 with the identical +3/+2/+1 partial ladder, 0, -1. SECTION 3 (Max 20) FIVE numerical value, two-decimal truncate/round, +4 / 0, no negative. SECTION 4 (Max 8) TWO (02) question stems, TWO (02) questions per stem (= 4 sub-questions), each a NUMERICAL VALUE, Full +2 / Zero 0 in all other cases. So Paper 1 = 48 Q and Paper 2 = 54 Q, both 180 marks, both 3 hours (P1 09:00-12:00, P2 14:30-17:30 on 17 May 2026), both compulsory. => IMPLICATION: Data model needs a 'question stem / shared passage' entity: one stem with N child questions, each independently scored. Mark totals must be derived by summing per-section max marks, never assumed to be questions x 4. Paper 1 and Paper 2 templates must be separate blueprint records.
- (high) JEE Advanced multi-correct negative marking changed from -2 to -1 between 2025 and 2026 — the pattern is explicitly not fixed: The 2026 Paper 1/2 Section 2 instructions read 'Negative Marks : -1 In all other cases'. JEE Advanced 2025 (IIT Kanpur) used -2 for multi-correct, and 2025 Paper 1 Section 2 had only 3 questions worth 12 marks vs 4 questions worth 16 in 2026. jeeadv.ac.in states only that 'The examination pattern for JEE (Advanced) 2026 will remain consistent with that of previous JEE (Advanced) examinations' — no numeric commitment; the exact counts and marking are disclosed only on-screen on exam day. Historically JEE Advanced has also used integer-answer (0-9), single-digit, paragraph/comprehension, and List-I/List-II matrix-match variants in different years. => IMPLICATION: Hardcoding Advanced marking is a guaranteed correctness bug within one year. Store marking as a versioned JSON rule object on the SECTION row (exam_year + paper + section -> rule), so a 2027 pattern shift is a data migration, not an app release. Also seed historical PYQ papers with their own year's rules so replayed PYQ mocks score authentically.
- (high) Exact partial-marking algorithm for multi-correct (the version most competitors get wrong): The rule is NOT 'marks proportional to correct options selected'. Let C = set of correct options, S = set of selected options. If S is empty -> 0. If S is not a subset of C (any wrong option selected) -> -1 (2026) / -2 (2025 and earlier), regardless of how many correct options were also selected. If S == C -> +4. Else (S is a proper non-empty subset of C): +3 only when |C| == 4 and |S| == 3; +2 when |C| >= 3 and |S| == 2; +1 when |C| >= 2 and |S| == 1. Worked example given verbatim in the paper: if (A),(B),(D) are the only correct answers, choosing A+B+D = +4; A+B = +2; A+D = +2; B+D = +2; A alone = +1; B alone = +1; D alone = +1; nothing = 0; any other combination = -1. Note the asymmetry: with |C| = 3, choosing 2 of 3 gives +2 (not +3), and there is no +3 tier at all unless all four options are correct. The widely circulated formula 'partial = 4 x selected_correct / total_correct' is WRONG (it would give 2.67 for 2-of-3). => IMPLICATION: Implement as a pure, unit-tested function scoreMultiCorrect(correctSet, selectedSet, rule) with the |C|/|S| lookup table, plus a golden-test suite built from the verbatim worked example in the 2026 paper. This is a headline differentiator: most competitor apps use the proportional formula and mis-score 2-of-3 cases.
- (high) NEET UG 2026: no Section A/B, 180 compulsory questions, 180 minutes, pen-and-paper OMR: Information Bulletin NEET (UG)-2026, Chapter on Pattern of the Test, verbatim: 'The question paper will consist of 180 compulsory questions which must be attempted by the candidates in 180 minutes. (As per Letter CDN-20011/289/2024-Cordination-NMC dated 05.12.2025)'. Table: Physics 45 Q / 180 marks, Chemistry 45 Q / 180 marks, Biology (Botany & Zoology) 90 Q / 360 marks; Total 180 Q / 720 marks. Duration 02:00 PM to 05:00 PM. Type: 'MCQ with four options and single correct answer/best option'. Marking: +4 correct/most appropriate, -1 any incorrect option, 0 unanswered. Mode: 'single day/ single shift exam in PEN and PAPER mode as was conducted in 2025'. 13 mediums (English, Hindi, Assamese, Bengali, Gujarati, Kannada, Malayalam, Marathi, Odia, Punjabi, Tamil, Telugu, Urdu). PwD/PwBD get +1 hour. The 200-minute / Section-A-35Q + Section-B-15Q-choose-10 pattern used 2021-2024 is dead — do not implement it for current mocks. => IMPLICATION: NEET mock engine is much simpler than JEE: single section per subject, no NVQ, no partial marking. But Biology must be split into Botany (45) and Zoology (45) sub-buckets for analytics even though the paper presents 90 Biology questions — that split is how students and coaching institutes reason about it and matches our Subject>Chapter>Topic hierarchy.
- (high) NEET's 'defective question' rules differ materially from JEE Main's and affect post-hoc rescoring: NEET UG 2026 bulletin verbatim: (i) if more than one option is found correct, +4 is awarded 'to only those who have marked any of the correct options'; (ii) if all options are found correct, +4 to all who ATTEMPTED the question; (iii) if none of the options is correct, or the question is wrong, or the question is dropped, +4 is given to ALL candidates who APPEARED 'irrespective of the fact whether the question has been attempted or not attempted'. JEE Main 2026 bulletin: dropped/incorrect question -> +4 to all candidates who appeared for that particular shift; if all options correct -> +4 to all who attempted. => IMPLICATION: Our scoring must support a post-publication 'answer key revision' event that recomputes historical attempts: per-question flags MULTI_KEY, ALL_CORRECT, DROPPED, each with its own award rule and a different eligible-population (attempted vs appeared). Build this as an idempotent Supabase Edge Function / SQL job with an audit trail, since it retroactively changes leaderboards and rewards.
- (high) NTA CBT console spec (Appendix-VIII, JEE Main 2026 IB) — the exact behaviours to clone: Verbatim/near-verbatim: total duration 180 min (Paper 1) / 210 min (Paper 2A, 2B). 'The clock will be set at the server. The countdown timer in the top right corner of screen will display the remaining time available... When the timer reaches zero, the examination will end by itself. You will not be required to end or submit your examination.' Question Palette on the RIGHT shows per-question status; collapsible with '>' and reopened with '<'. Navigation: (a) clicking a question number in the palette goes there directly and 'does NOT save your answer to the current question'; (b) Save & Next saves then advances; (c) Mark for Review & Next saves, marks for review, then advances. Deselect an MCQ option by clicking it again or clicking Clear Response. To change an already-saved answer: select the question from the palette, click Clear Response, then re-answer. Sections are displayed on the TOP BAR; clicking a section name views it; the current section is highlighted; 'After clicking the Save and Next button on the last question for a Section, you will automatically be taken to the first question of the next section'; 'You can shuffle between sections and questions anytime during the examination'. A per-section summary legend appears above the palette. 'Question Paper' icon at top right shows the full paper. Profile image at top right switches the paper's language mid-exam; English is authoritative for evaluation. The physical keyboard is DISABLED for the entire duration — all input via mouse and on-screen virtual keyboard. Accessibility: dark/light mode toggle, font size increase/decrease, cursor trail toggle, cursor size, and a draggable Screen Magnifier. Blank rough sheets with a name/roll header are provided and must be surrendered. On computer/mouse malfunction, lost time is adjusted at the server. => IMPLICATION: This is our UI spec, verbatim. Key server-authoritative requirements: (1) timer anchored to server clock with drift correction and auto-submit at 0; (2) palette-click must NOT persist the in-progress answer — that single behaviour is what makes mocks feel real and is the most commonly missed detail; (3) auto-advance to next section on Save&Next at a section's last question; (4) free section switching (JEE Main/Advanced have no section locking, unlike CAT/GATE); (5) time-loss compensation implies our server must support extending a single attempt's deadline (useful for app crash / network loss on mobile).
- (medium) Question palette has exactly five states, with a non-obvious evaluation rule for 'Marked for Review': Bulletin points 20-21: five statuses. Standard NTA legend (rendered as images in the PDF, so labels here are from the surrounding text plus the widely reproduced NTA legend): (1) You have not visited the question yet — grey/white; (2) You have not answered the question — red; (3) You have answered the question — green; (4) You have NOT answered the question but have marked it for review — purple/violet; (5) You have answered the question but marked it for review — purple with a green tick. Verbatim rule: 'A candidate has the option of answering a question and simultaneously Marked for Review, these answers WILL be considered for evaluation. However, if a candidate has simply put Marked for Review for a question without answering it, the corresponding question marked for review without an answer will NOT be considered for evaluation.' Also: 'The Marked for Review status for a question simply indicates that you would like to look at that question again.' Exact hex colours are not published by NTA (unverified) — the semantics, not the exact hex, are the fidelity requirement. => IMPLICATION: Model attempt state as a tuple (visited: bool, answer: nullable, marked_for_review: bool) and derive the 5 palette states, rather than storing a single enum — this makes state 5 (answered + marked) fall out correctly and guarantees answered+marked responses are scored. Given our 'no emoji / professional' design bar, pick a deliberate accessible palette (state must also be distinguishable by shape/number badge, not colour alone) but keep the same five semantics and the same legend wording.
- (high) Calculators are BANNED in JEE Main 2026 — the on-screen calculator line in the bulletin was an NTA typo: NTA Public Notice dated 02 November 2025, verbatim: 'In the Appendix-VIII of the Information Bulletin for JEE (Main) - 2026 on Computer Based Test (CBT)... it has been mentioned that an onscreen standard calculator will be available during the Computer-Based Test (CBT). However, this feature forms part of the generic test conducting platform and does NOT apply to JEE (Main) conducted by the NTA, as the use of calculators in any form is not permitted in this examination. The revised Information Bulletin for JEE (Main) 2026 has been uploaded... NTA regrets the typographic error.' JEE Advanced likewise prohibits calculators; candidates get a scribble pad and only an on-screen virtual NUMERIC KEYPAD for entering numerical answers. NEET is OMR with no electronic aids (electronic watches with calculator facility are explicitly barred). => IMPLICATION: Do NOT ship an in-test calculator for JEE Main, JEE Advanced or NEET mocks. Ship instead: (a) an on-screen virtual numeric keypad component for NVQ entry (mouse/tap only, matching NTA), and (b) an optional digital scratchpad. Note the bulletin PDF still hosted at the October 2025 CDN path contains the erroneous calculator text — always cross-check bulletins against the Public Notices feed.
- (high) JEE Main normalisation: percentile of the TOTAL raw score, per shift, to 7 decimal places: Appendix-VII, JEE (Main) 2026 IB. Percentile Score = 100 x (number of candidates who appeared in that Shift with raw score EQUAL TO OR LESS than the candidate) / (total number of candidates who appeared in that Shift). Topper of every shift gets exactly 100. 'The Percentile Scores will be calculated up to 7 decimal places to avoid the bunching effect and reduce ties.' Explicit warning: 'The Percentile of the Total shall NOT be an aggregate or average of the Percentile of the individual subject. The percentile score is not the same as the percentage of marks obtained.' Step-3: percentile scores of the Total Raw Score across all shifts are merged and called the NTA score.
