PITR gives a worst-case RPO of 2 minutes because WAL is backed up at 2-minute intervals, and "the project becomes inaccessible during restoration" with downtime proportional to database size - Supabase does not publish an RTO number (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/backups). Free tier has no PITR at all; Pro daily backups retain only 7 days. Restoring the whole project to fix one table means discarding every legitimate write made since the incident, i.e. 10,000 students' in-flight tests. -> FIX: 1) Enable PITR (Pro add-on, ~$100/mo for 7-day, ~$200 for 14-day, ~$400 for 28-day retention) before launch; Supabase's prod checklist recommends it above 4 GB. 2) Never restore the whole project for a single-table incident - instead restore to a NEW project at the target timestamp, then selectively copy the damaged table back via COPY/postgres_fdw. Rehearse this and record the measured RTO for your actual data size; do not assume. 3) Independent logical backups: nightly `pg_dump` of the question bank + answer keys to a separate GCS bucket with object versioning and a different credential domain, so a compromised Supabase account cannot destroy backups. 4) Make destructive access hard: no direct psql to prod for application engineers; all bulk writes through reviewed migrations or an admin RPC with a dry-run mode and row-count assertion.
- [critical/client] EC-DATA-04: A student on patchy 4G loses connectivity for 25 minutes mid-test, or the app is killed by Android's low-memory killer at question 62. -> FAILS: Total loss of 2 hours of work if answers are only held in React state and flushed at submit. This is the most common real-world support complaint for exam apps in India, where connectivity is the norm-breaking variable, and it directly causes uninstalls and refund demands. -> FIX: Offline-first attempt state. Every answer selection writes synchronously to MMKV/SQLite keyed by `attempt_id`, plus a monotonically increasing local `revision`. A background sync flushes deltas every 30-60s (batched with the heartbeat, EC-HERD-07) and retries with jitter. On relaunch the app rehydrates from local storage and reconciles with the server by taking `max(revision)` per question. The server timer keeps running (the student loses time, which is correct and must be stated clearly in the UI). Because all 90 questions and images were prefetched at admission (EC-HERD-03), a student can complete an entire paper fully offline and sync at the end - design for this explicitly.
- [high/infra] EC-COST-01: Question images are 800 KB PNGs of LaTeX-rendered problems, served without CDN caching (see EC-LEAK-02 signed-URL pathology), across 12 mock tests a month. -> FAILS: Egress blowout. 90 images x 800 KB = 72 MB per paper per student. 10,000 students x 12 tests = 8.6 TB/month of origin egress. Pro includes 250 GB, overage is $0.09/GB (https://supabase.com/pricing) -> ~8,350 GB over -> ~$750/month from images alone, on top of a $25 plan. Cache-busting signed URLs make CDN hit rate ~0%, so none of it is absorbed at the edge. -> FIX: 1) Fix the caching pathology first (EC-LEAK-02 / EC-HERD-03) - a 99% edge hit rate turns 8.6 TB of origin egress into ~90 GB. 2) Prefer rich-text + client-side LaTeX rendering (KaTeX in a WebView or react-native-mathjax) over images wherever the admin authoring flow allows; a LaTeX string is ~1 KB vs 800 KB, a 3-order-of-magnitude reduction. 3) Where images are unavoidable, enforce WebP/AVIF at upload with a max dimension of 1200px and a 150 KB budget, rejecting oversized uploads in the admin RPC (Storage global file size limit is configurable per plan up to 500 GB on Pro/Team - https://supabase.com/docs/guides/storage/uploads/file-limits, so the platform will NOT stop an admin from uploading a 20 MB scan). 4) Put Cloudflare in front and serve images from a Cloudflare-cached hostname so egress is billed at Cloudflare's (zero, on standard plans) rather than Supabase's $0.09/GB. 5) Alarm on daily egress > 2x trailing median.
- [high/infra] EC-COST-02: An Edge Function that grades a submission calls another Edge Function (or itself) on failure, and a malformed payload makes it fail deterministically. Or a webhook retries forever on a 500. -> FAILS: Runaway invocation loop. Supabase documents roughly 5,000 requests/minute for recursive function calls (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/functions/limits); Pro/Team include 2M invocations with overage at $2 per additional million (https://supabase.com/pricing). A loop at 5,000/min generates 7.2M invocations/day -> ~$14/day and rising, and saturates the function runtime so legitimate grading fails. Combined with the 400s wall-clock and 2s CPU limits, a stuck function also holds capacity. -> FIX: 1) No function may invoke itself or another function without a decrementing `x-retry-depth` header, hard-capped at 3. 2) Move all retryable work to pgmq with a bounded `read_ct` - archive to a dead-letter queue after 5 reads rather than retrying forever (https://github.com/pgmq/pgmq). 3) Poison-message handling: any payload that fails twice with the same error goes to DLQ, never back to the main queue. 4) Circuit breaker on external calls with a token-bucket retry budget. 5) Billing alerts + Pro spend cap left ON as a hard backstop, with the understanding that hitting the cap degrades service - so alarm well before it. 6) Per-function invocation-rate dashboards with a page at 3x baseline.
- [high/db] EC-COST-03: An admin analytics screen runs an unbounded aggregate (`SELECT ... GROUP BY topic` over 90M answer rows) at 10:15 while a test is live, or a student-facing "all India rank" query does a full sort per request. -> FAILS: One expensive query consumes the shared CPU and IO budget of the compute instance; effective IOPS is the lower of what the compute size supports and what the disk is provisioned for (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/compute-and-disk). Every student request queues behind it. Supabase's own read-replica guidance treats sustained CPU > 70% as the threshold for structural change (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/read-replicas). -> FIX: 1) Separate roles and separate budgets: `statement_timeout = 5s` for `authenticated`, `30s` for the admin reporting role, `2s` for `anon`. 2) Route all ADMIN analytics to a read replica (note the documented limitation: Auth, Storage and Realtime cannot use replicas), or to a nightly-refreshed materialised view / BigQuery export on GCP - never live aggregates over the answers table. 3) Pre-compute ranks and percentiles at grading time (EC-HERD-08) so student-facing rank is an indexed point lookup. 4) `pg_stat_statements` review in every sprint; alarm on any statement whose total_exec_time enters the top 5. 5) Block admin analytics entirely during live test windows via a feature flag.

## edge-compliance (Compliance, privacy, platform-policy and business edge cases for an Indian JEE/NEET edtech app with minor (under-18) users — DPDP Act/Rules, app-store policy, payments, accessibility, content IP, and operational duty of care)
- [critical/legal] EC-DPDP-01: A 16-year-old JEE aspirant self-registers with a phone number/Google sign-in and starts a mock test. No parent is involved. Under DPDPA 2023 s.2(f) a 'child' is anyone under 18, and s.9(1) requires verifiable parental consent BEFORE any processing. DPDP Rules 2025 Rule 10 (notified 13 Nov 2025, G.S.R. 846(E)) requires 'appropriate technical and organisational measures' to confirm the consenting person is an identifiable adult — via identity/age details already held, details voluntarily supplied, a virtual token from an authorised entity, or a Digital Locker service provider. A tick-box 'I am 18+' or 'my parent consents' is explicitly insufficient. Majority of the target user base (JEE/NEET aspirants in classes 11-12 and one-year droppers) is 16-18, so this is the DEFAULT path, not an edge case. -> FAILS: Every student record created without VPC is unlawfully processed from day one. Exposure is up to Rs 200 crore for breach of children's-data obligations (DPDP Schedule). Retrospective cure is near-impossible: you cannot re-consent a user base you cannot contact through a verified adult. Data Protection Board can also order erasure, which would wipe attempt history, analytics and leaderboards. -> FIX: Make VPC a hard gate in the signup state machine, not a post-hoc banner. (1) Collect DOB first on a neutral, non-suggestive age screen (no 'must be 18+' prompt that teaches users to lie). (2) If DOB < 18, block ALL processing beyond the minimum needed to run the consent flow and route to a parent-verification flow: parent enters own mobile+DOB, OTP, then a Digital Locker (DigiLocker) virtual-token exchange or an authorised age-token provider to prove adulthood. (3) Persist an append-only consent ledger row: child_id, parent_id, verification method, notice version id, purpose scope, timestamp, IP/device, withdrawal history. Store in Postgres with RLS deny-by-default and an immutable audit table (no UPDATE/DELETE grants; trigger-enforced). (4) Gate every RLS policy on a `vpc_status = 'verified'` claim so an unverified minor literally cannot SELECT question content. (5) Budget for verification failure: build a fallback manual review queue for parents without DigiLocker. Sources: https://www.dpdpa.com/dpdparules/rule10.html , https://dataclyr.com/dpdp-resources/dpdp-playbooks/dpdp-for-edtech/verifiable-parental-consent/
- [critical/legal] EC-DPDP-02: Product/legal assumes the Fourth Schedule 'educational institution' exemption applies, so VPC and the tracking ban are skipped. Fourth Schedule Part A does exempt educational institutions for 'tracking and behavioural monitoring for the educational activities of such institution; or in the interests of safety of children enrolled with such institution' — but the exemption is entity-based and purpose-bound, and 'educational institution' is not defined in the Rules in a way that has been read to cover commercial learning apps. -> FAILS: A commercial B2C app is almost certainly NOT an 'educational institution'. Relying on the exemption means the entire consent architecture is void. Second-order trap: even if the exemption applied, it exempts only parts of s.9(3) (tracking/behavioural monitoring) for enumerated purposes — it does NOT exempt s.9(1) verifiable parental consent. Two independent failure vectors from one wrong assumption. -> FIX: Assume zero exemption. Treat the app as a fully-obligated Data Fiduciary under s.9(1) and s.9(3). If a B2B2C school/coaching channel is later added, the school is a separate Data Fiduciary and you become a Data Processor for that flow — write a distinct DPA, a distinct tenant in the schema, and do NOT commingle the B2C and B2B consent bases. Only claim the exemption after a written legal opinion and only for the school-sourced tenant. Sources: https://www.dpdpa.com/schedule/schedule4.html , https://ksandk.com/data-protection-and-data-privacy/childrens-data-protection-under-indias-dpdp-rules/ , https://www.privybyidfy.com/blog/dpdpa-for-schools-and-edtechs-the-2026-guide-to-childrens-data-compliance
- [critical/product] EC-DPDP-03: The rewards layer ships: streaks, daily-login coins, leaderboards, 'you are slipping vs peers' nudges, push notifications timed to re-engage, and an ML model that recommends the next topic based on behavioural telemetry. All users are children. DPDPA s.9(3) prohibits 'tracking or behavioural monitoring of children or targeted advertising directed at children'. The Rules do not define where pedagogy ends and engagement optimisation begins. -> FAILS: Engagement-optimisation telemetry (session length, notification response rate, time-of-day activity, streak-loss-aversion loops) reads as behavioural monitoring of a child. Public leaderboards additionally collide with the Supreme Court's July 2025 mental-health directions. Penalty exposure up to Rs 200 crore, plus a plausible Board order to strip the feature — which would be a product-defining amputation shipped late. -> FIX: Split telemetry into two physically separate pipelines at ingest. PEDAGOGICAL (defensible): per-question correctness, time-per-question, topic mastery, syllabus coverage — documented pedagogical purpose, disclosed in the notice, parent-visible, parent-toggleable. ENGAGEMENT (not defensible for minors): retention scoring, churn prediction, notification A/B optimisation, session-length maximisation, any profile used to time a nudge. Disable the second pipeline entirely for under-18 accounts at the API gateway, not in the client. Make leaderboards opt-in, pseudonymous (display handle, never real name/school), and scoped to a self-selected cohort rather than global rank. Never ship an ad SDK. Record the pedagogical-purpose justification per event type in a reviewable register — it is the artefact you will be asked for. Source on the defensible-zone framing: https://www.privybyidfy.com/blog/dpdpa-for-schools-and-edtechs-the-2026-guide-to-childrens-data-compliance
- [high/legal] EC-DPDP-04: MeitY's Feb 2026 consultation proposes compressing the DPDP compliance runway from 18 months to 12 — moving the full-obligation date from 13 May 2027 to 13 November 2026, notifying Significant Data Fiduciaries 'as soon as possible' rather than waiting, enforcing Rule 13(4)/Rule 15 cross-border restrictions immediately on notification, and requiring retention rules within 90 days of the amendment. Stakeholder feedback closed 4 Feb 2026. -> FAILS: A roadmap that back-loads consent, erasure and DPO work to late 2026/early 2027 on the assumption of a May 2027 deadline is invalidated overnight when the amendment notifies. Cross-border restrictions taking effect 'immediately' would strand any architecture that has already shipped with non-Indian subprocessors. -> FIX: Plan to the 13 Nov 2026 date, not 13 May 2027, and treat any slippage as free margin. Concretely: VPC + consent ledger + erasure + breach runbook must be production-complete before the first 1,000 real users, not before a legal deadline. Track the MeitY amendment as a named risk with a monthly check on meity.gov.in. [confidence: medium — the compressed timeline is a PROPOSAL as of the Feb 2026 reporting and is unverified as finally notified; the notified position remains 13 May 2027.] Sources: https://ssrana.in/articles/meity-plans-to-cut-short-dpdp-compliance-timeline-and-notify-cross-border-restrictions-for-sdfs/ , https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_Personal_Data_Protection_Act,_2023
- [critical/infra] EC-DPDP-05: A Supabase Storage bucket holding admin-uploaded question images is misconfigured (public bucket or over-broad RLS), or a service-role key leaks from an Edge Function env, exposing student PII and attempt data. Rule 7 requires: intimate each affected Data Principal 'without delay' with breach description, consequences and mitigation; give the Data Protection Board an initial intimation without delay AND a detailed report within 72 hours (extendable on request). -> FAILS: For a child user base, 'intimate the affected Data Principal' means reaching the verified PARENT, not the student — and you can only do that if you captured and maintained a verified parent contact channel. Without it, the 72-hour clock expires with no lawful way to notify. Exposure up to Rs 250 crore (security safeguards failure) plus up to Rs 200 crore (notification failure), stackable. -> FIX: (1) Store a verified parent email/mobile as a first-class, always-current field, re-verified annually; treat a stale parent contact as a compliance defect with its own alert. (2) Pre-write the breach runbook with a named owner, a 72-hour timer, and templated Board and parent notices in English + the user's chosen Eighth Schedule language. (3) Technical: default-deny RLS on every table, no public Storage buckets (signed URLs with short TTL only), service-role key never in the mobile bundle or in a client-callable Edge Function, Postgres audit logging retained per Rule 6, and automated CI checks that fail the build on a table without RLS enabled. (4) Rehearse the notification path quarterly — the failure is almost always organisational, not technical. Source: https://www.dpdp.ind.in/rules.php
- [high/db] EC-DPDP-06: A parent invokes the right to erasure (DPDPA s.12, Rule 14) for their child mid-test-series, or a student withdraws consent the night before a scheduled mock. Withdrawal must be as easy as giving consent. Meanwhile the business needs attempt logs for leaderboard integrity, dispute resolution, and to defend a rank challenge. -> FAILS: Naive hard-DELETE cascades destroy leaderboard integrity, invalidate percentile calculations for every other student in that test, and orphan Redis sorted-set entries — corrupting ranks for 10,000 concurrent users. Naive refusal to delete is a s.12 violation. Withdrawal mid-timed-test with an authoritative server timer creates a state the timer service has no defined transition for. -> FIX: Design erasure as a two-tier operation. Tier 1 (identity): purge/crypto-shred all direct and indirect identifiers — name, phone, email, DOB, device ids, parent record, notes, uploaded images. Tier 2 (statistical): retain de-identified, non-rejoinable aggregate score contributions under a documented lawful basis so percentile maths stays sound; the row must be irreversibly severed from any identifier (drop the mapping key, do not merely null the column). Define the state machine explicitly: consent withdrawal during an active attempt → finalise/void the attempt per a published rule, release the server timer lease, remove the Redis leaderboard member by member-id, then run erasure asynchronously with an idempotent job. Publish the erasure SLA and the exact retained-data description in the notice. Source: https://www.dpdp.ind.in/rules.php
- [medium/product] EC-DPDP-07: A student who signed up at 17 under parental consent turns 18 during a 12-month test-series subscription. The lawful basis for processing was the parent's verifiable consent; the parent also retains visibility into the now-adult user's performance data. -> FAILS: Post-18, the parent's consent is no longer the correct basis and continued parent visibility into an adult's data is itself an unlawful disclosure. Silently continuing is a s.6/s.9 defect; silently cutting the parent off mid-subscription breaks a paying customer's expectations. Nobody builds this transition and it fires for a large cohort every year in a JEE/NEET product. -> FIX: Run a scheduled job on DOB. At T-30 days notify both parties. On the 18th birthday: suspend child-mode restrictions, force a fresh first-party consent capture from the now-adult user on next login (blocking, with the current notice version), and default parent data-visibility to OFF pending the adult's explicit re-grant. Keep the payment relationship separate from the data relationship — the parent can keep paying without keeping visibility. Log the basis transition in the consent ledger as a distinct event type.
- [medium/api] EC-DPDP-08: Rule 4 (Consent Manager registration) commences 13 Nov 2025 + 12 months = 13 Nov 2026. Consent Managers must register with the Board, meet Schedule I conditions including a minimum net worth of Rs 2 crore, provide an interoperable platform, and retain consent records/audit trails for at least 7 years. Data Principals may choose to route consent through a registered Consent Manager rather than through your UI. -> FAILS: If the app hard-codes its own consent UI as the only path, it cannot honour consent granted, reviewed or withdrawn through a third-party Consent Manager once that ecosystem is live. Withdrawals arriving via a Consent Manager that the app cannot ingest become silent unlawful-processing incidents. -> FIX: Model consent as an external, versioned, event-sourced resource from the start: a `consent_events` table keyed by (principal_id, purpose, notice_version, source), where `source` can be 'first_party' or a consent-manager id. Expose an authenticated webhook/API to accept grant and withdrawal events. Retain consent records 7 years even though that exceeds your business retention — set a separate retention class so the general erasure job does not sweep them. Do NOT plan to become a Consent Manager (Rs 2 crore net worth, registration, conflict-of-interest bar). Source: https://www.dpdp.ind.in/rules.php
- [high/legal] EC-DPDP-09: At 10,000+ concurrent students and growth, the company may be notified as a Significant Data Fiduciary under s.10 read with Rule 13 — triggered by volume and sensitivity of data, risk of harm, and other notified factors. Thresholds are NOT yet formally notified; MeitY signalled in Feb 2026 an intent to notify SDFs early. -> FAILS: SDF designation adds, on short notice: an India-resident Data Protection Officer, an annual DPIA, an annual independent audit reported to the Board, algorithmic due diligence on software that could pose risks to Data Principal rights, and compliance with government restrictions on cross-border transfer of specified data categories. Penalty up to Rs 150 crore. A child-heavy user base materially raises 'risk of harm' and therefore designation odds. Algorithmic due diligence would bite the randomisation engine, adaptive difficulty and any recommendation model. -> FIX: Pre-build the artefacts even if not designated: (a) name a DPO-equivalent resident in India and publish the contact (Rule 9 contact-information duty applies regardless); (b) run a DPIA before shipping any new profiling/adaptive feature and keep it versioned in the repo; (c) document the randomisation and difficulty-selection algorithms in plain language with a fairness note (seeded per-student shuffle must be reproducible and auditable — store the seed, do not regenerate); (d) keep an up-to-date subprocessor register. Sources: https://ksandk.com/data-protection-and-data-privacy/dpdp-act-2023-rules-for-significant-data-fiduciaries/ , https://ssrana.in/articles/meity-plans-to-cut-short-dpdp-compliance-timeline-and-notify-cross-border-restrictions-for-sdfs/
- [medium/product] EC-DPDP-10: Rule 3 requires that every consent request be accompanied by a clear, plain-language notice presented independently of any other information, itemising the data collected, the purposes, the services enabled, and how to withdraw and exercise rights — and available in English or ANY language in the Eighth Schedule to the Constitution (22 languages). The user base is pan-India, heavily Hindi/regional-language. -> FAILS: An English-only consent notice is a Rule 3 defect and, practically, means the parent did not understand what they consented to — which undermines the validity of the VPC itself. Bundling the privacy notice into the Terms of Service scroll ('presented independently') is a second defect. Translating LaTeX-heavy product copy is trivial; translating and version-controlling a legal notice across 22 languages with per-user language selection is not. -> FIX: Treat the notice as a versioned content artefact, not a static screen: `notice_versions(id, lang, body, effective_from)`. Record the exact (notice_version_id, lang) shown at the moment of consent in the consent ledger — this is the evidence that survives audit. Ship English + Hindi at launch and add languages by user-base share; offer a language picker BEFORE the notice, not after. Present the notice on its own screen with its own accept action, never inside a combined 'I agree to Terms & Privacy' checkbox. Source: https://www.dpdp.ind.in/rules.php
- [high/infra] EC-LOC-01: Supabase project region is chosen at creation. ap-south-1 (Mumbai) is available. Region cannot be changed after project creation without a full migration to a new project. Rule 15 permits transfer outside India subject to whatever the Central Government specifies; Rule 13(4) allows additional localisation constraints on specified categories for SDFs, and MeitY signalled these could be enforced immediately on notification. -> FAILS: Provisioning in ap-southeast-1 (Singapore) or a 'general' region for convenience creates an unfixable-in-place localisation and latency problem. If a localisation notification lands, remediation means a live migration of the Postgres primary, Storage objects, Auth users (password hashes and refresh tokens) and Realtime state for a live user base mid-test-season. Latency also matters independently: Singapore adds roughly 40-70 ms RTT versus Mumbai for Indian users, which compounds on a server-authoritative timer and on 10k concurrent submissions. -> FIX: Provision in ap-south-1 (Mumbai) on day one and record the decision. Note the general regions do not support read replicas or Management API region control. Keep every stateful dependency co-located: Redis in an Indian region (leaderboards, randomisation seeds, timer leases), GCP auxiliary services in asia-south1 (Mumbai) or asia-south2 (Delhi), backups in-country. Maintain a written subprocessor/data-flow map with the region of each. If any component must sit abroad, ensure only de-identified data crosses. Sources: https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/regions , https://supabase.com/docs/guides/troubleshooting/change-project-region-eWJo5Z
- [high/client] EC-LOC-02: Third-party SDKs and services leak child data out of the country and out of the consent scope: crash reporting, product analytics, push notification providers, error tracking, and the YouTube solution links (which load the YouTube player and set Google cookies/identifiers as soon as a 16-year-old taps 'Watch solution'). -> FAILS: Embedding a standard YouTube player transmits identifiers to Google and may serve interest-based advertising to a child — squarely into the s.9(3) targeted-advertising prohibition and, on Android, into Google Play Families' ban on transmitting AAID from children or unknown-age users. Analytics SDKs typically collect device identifiers by default. Each is a separate unlawful transfer with no lawful basis and no disclosure in the notice. -> FIX: (1) Never embed the standard YouTube player for an under-18 session. Either open the link in the external browser/YouTube app after an explicit interstitial (making it the user's own act), or use youtube-nocookie.com in a WebView with no persistent storage — and disclose it. Prefer hosting solution videos yourself in Supabase Storage/GCS for the ones you control. (2) Audit every SDK for identifier collection; disable AAID/IDFA collection globally, not conditionally. (3) Zero ad SDKs, ever — the monetisation model is subscriptions, so there is no reason to accept the risk. (4) Maintain a subprocessor register with region and purpose per vendor and reflect it in the Rule 3 notice. Sources: https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/9893335?hl=en , https://www.dpdpa.com/dpdparules/rule10.html
- [high/product] EC-PLAY-01: Google Play Console requires a target-audience declaration before publishing. Declaring 13-17 (accurate for JEE/NEET aspirants) pulls the app into Google Play Families policies. Mixed-audience apps (teens + adult droppers/parents) must implement a neutral age screen. Play reserves the right to override a declared audience based on actual content and design, and Play Age Signals API is in beta (as of 20 July 2026) returning age bracket, parental-supervision status and verification status for covered regions. -> FAILS: Declaring 18+ to dodge Families policy while the store listing, screenshots and content obviously target class 11/12 students triggers a Google override, policy strike, and removal — typically discovered at the worst moment (a release during exam season). Declaring 13-17 honestly but shipping a non-certified ads SDK, requesting location, or transmitting AAID is an immediate Families violation. Play Age Signals is explicitly NOT verifiable parental consent and its ToS forbids using the signal for analytics, profiling or marketing. -> FIX: Declare 13-17 honestly. Implement a neutral age screen (no leading copy, no pre-filled adult DOB, remembered so it is not farmable by retry). Ship zero ads and zero third-party ad SDKs; do not request ACCESS_FINE_LOCATION or read phone number via TelephonyManager; do not transmit AAID, IMEI, IMSI, MAC, SSID, BSSID, Build Serial or SIM Serial. Complete the Data Safety declaration to match the actual SDK behaviour (mismatches are a common rejection cause). Use Play Age Signals only to shape age-appropriate experience — never as your VPC mechanism, and never feed it into analytics. Sources: https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/9893335?hl=en , https://developer.android.com/google/play/age-signals/overview
- [high/product] EC-PLAY-02: Apple restructured App Store age ratings, adding 13+, 16+ and 18+ tiers alongside a new questionnaire covering in-app controls, capabilities (e.g. unrestricted web access), user-generated content and messaging. Developers must answer the updated questions in App Store Connect; the stated deadline is 31 January 2026 to avoid interruption when submitting updates. Guideline 2.3.6 requires honest answers. -> FAILS: Unanswered questionnaire blocks app update submissions entirely — a hard stop on shipping hotfixes. Answering dishonestly to get a lower rating risks removal and, per Apple, regulator inquiry. Two specific traps for this product: the YouTube solution links are arguably 'unrestricted web access' (rating-raising), and student notes plus any admin-authored free text can read as user-generated content. -> FIX: Answer the questionnaire before any release train depends on it; re-answer whenever the YouTube-link behaviour, notes sharing or any messaging feature changes. If YouTube links open externally with an interstitial rather than in an in-app browser, document that in the answer. Keep notes strictly private-to-self (no sharing, no comments) at launch — this keeps you out of the UGC bucket and out of Guideline 1.2. Sources: https://developer.apple.com/news/upcoming-requirements/?id=07242025a , https://developer.apple.com/app-store/review/guidelines/
- [high/product] EC-PLAY-03: Someone proposes listing the app in Apple's Kids Category for discoverability among students, or uses 'For Students'/child-implying terms in metadata. Guideline 1.3 forbids Kids Category apps from containing links out of the app, purchasing opportunities or other distractions unless behind a parental gate, and bars third-party analytics and third-party advertising. Guideline 5.1.4 confirms a parental gate is NOT the same as parental consent to collect personal data. Guideline 2.3.8 reserves 'For Kids'/'For Children' terms to the Kids Category. -> FAILS: Kids Category selection is a one-way door: 'once customers expect your app to follow the Kids Category requirements, it will need to continue to meet these guidelines in subsequent updates, even if you decide to deselect the category.' YouTube solution links (links out) and subscription purchase flows both violate 1.3 unless gated. The product would be permanently crippled. -> FIX: Do NOT opt into the Kids Category. Target 13-17 via honest age rating instead. Independently, still ship a parental gate in front of the purchase flow and external links — it is good practice for a minor user base and is what a regulator will look for — but do not take on the category's structural constraints. Keep 'kids'/'children' out of app name, subtitle, icon, screenshots and description. Source: https://developer.apple.com/app-store/review/guidelines/
- [critical/product] EC-PAY-01: The team integrates Razorpay/PhonePe directly in-app for test-series subscriptions because Indian users prefer UPI and the payment-gateway fee (~2%) is far below store commission. Apple Guideline 3.1.1 mandates In-App Purchase to unlock features, functionality, subscriptions, in-game currencies or premium content, and forbids own mechanisms. Outside the US storefront, apps may not include buttons, external links or calls to action directing customers to other purchasing mechanisms, absent a StoreKit External Purchase Link Entitlement. -> FAILS: Guaranteed iOS rejection, and repeated attempts risk developer-account action. The 'reader app' escape does not apply — this app unlocks functionality. Any in-app text saying 'subscribe on our website for less' is itself a violation in the India storefront. Store commission (15% small-business / 30%) plus 18% GST materially changes unit economics for a price-sensitive Indian student market and must be modelled before pricing is set, not after. -> FIX: iOS: use StoreKit auto-renewable subscriptions (period >= 7 days, must work across all the user's devices per 3.1.2(a)). Android: use Google Play Billing, and in India additionally offer alternative billing under user-choice billing (see EC-PAY-02). Sell the annual/long plan on the WEB via Razorpay with no in-app mention or link, and honour it in-app via account entitlement — Guideline 3.1.3(b) permits accessing content acquired elsewhere provided the items are also available as IAP. Price the store SKUs with commission + GST baked in; do not present the web price inside the app. Source: https://developer.apple.com/app-store/review/guidelines/
- [medium/product] EC-PAY-02: Google Play in India permits alternative billing alongside Play billing, with the Play service fee reduced by 4% when the user chooses the alternative. Requirements: integrate the alternative billing APIs (or manually report transactions monthly by the 5th business day), report transactions within 24 hours via API, keep Play's billing system as an option, and maintain PCI DSS certification and fraud reporting. Separately, CCI's order against Google's Play billing was upheld by NCLAT in 2025 with the penalty reduced to Rs 216 crore; Google appealed to the Supreme Court, which admitted the appeal in 2025. -> FAILS: Building the payments architecture on an assumption about how the Supreme Court will rule is a bet with a multi-year settlement horizon. Meanwhile, the 4% reduction rarely covers the ~2% PSP fee plus reconciliation, refund-handling and PCI DSS burden, so 'alternative billing saves money' is often false at small scale. Manual monthly transaction reporting is an operational trap that produces silent policy breaches. -> FIX: Ship Play Billing only at launch; treat user-choice billing as a phase-2 optimisation gated on volume where 4% actually exceeds the fully-loaded cost of running a second rail. If adopted, integrate the alternative billing API (never manual reporting) and wire the 24-hour reporting into the same transaction-commit path so it cannot drift. Track the Supreme Court appeal as an assumption, not a plan. [confidence: medium — the Supreme Court outcome is unresolved as of this research and the current-2026 status is unverified beyond the appeal being admitted in 2025.] Sources: https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/13306652?hl=en , https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/13821247?hl=en , https://www.medianama.com/2025/07/223-google-play-store-billing-supreme-court-cci/
- [high/api] EC-PAY-03: Monthly subscription via UPI AutoPay or card e-mandate for web purchases. RBI's consolidated e-mandate framework (Master Direction issued 21 April 2026) requires AFA at e-mandate registration and modification, requires a pre-transaction notification to the customer at least 24 hours before each debit (with merchant name, amount, debit timing, mandate reference and opt-out), exempts recurring debits up to Rs 15,000 per transaction from AFA (Rs 1,00,000 for insurance premiums, mutual fund subscriptions and credit card bills — categories that do NOT include edtech), and forbids levying any charge on the customer for the e-mandate facility. -> FAILS: A test-series annual plan priced above Rs 15,000 forces a per-debit AFA step, collapsing renewal success rates. Missing or late 24-hour pre-debit notification makes the debit non-compliant and invites chargebacks and PSP penalties. Card e-mandates additionally fail on card expiry/reissue; UPI AutoPay mandates fail on VPA change. At exam-season scale, a batch of failed renewals lands exactly when students most need access. -> FIX: Price recurring SKUs at or below Rs 15,000 per debit (e.g. quarterly or monthly instalments rather than a single Rs 18,000 annual auto-debit); take anything above Rs 15,000 as a one-time payment, not a mandate. Prefer UPI AutoPay over card SI for Indian consumers (real-time mandate activation vs 2-5 business days for e-NACH; no card-expiry failure mode). Implement the 24-hour pre-debit notification as a scheduled job with retries and a dead-letter queue; treat a missed notification as a blocker that cancels the debit, not a warning. Build dunning: notify, retry, grace period with read-only access rather than instant lockout mid-syllabus. Never pass the mandate cost to the customer. Sources: https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/BS_ViewMasDirections.aspx?id=13374 , https://razorpay.com/blog/master-recurring-payments-upi-autopay-guide/
- [high/legal] EC-PAY-04: A 17-year-old completes a purchase using their own UPI ID or a card, and later the parent disputes it — or the student disputes it themselves. Under the Indian Contract Act 1872 s.11, a minor is not competent to contract and an agreement with a minor is void ab initio. -> FAILS: The subscription contract with the minor is void and unenforceable; the terms of service, the arbitration clause, the limitation of liability and the refund policy are all unenforceable against that user. Chargebacks succeed. Simultaneously, the platform has collected money from a person legally incapable of agreeing to the terms under which it was collected — a consumer-protection and reputational problem on top of the contractual one. -> FIX: Structure the commercial relationship so the PARENT is the contracting party and the STUDENT is the named beneficiary/end user — the same verified adult who gave VPC. Terms of service acceptance must be captured from the parent account with the same evidentiary rigour as consent. Where the store handles payment (IAP), rely on Apple Ask to Buy / Google Play parental approval as an additional layer but do not treat it as sufficient. Keep the payer identity and the learner identity as distinct entities in the data model from day one — retrofitting this is a schema-wide change. [confidence: medium — the contract-law position is well established; the specific interaction with store-mediated IAP for minors is unverified.]
- [critical/product] EC-REWARD-01: The rewards layer ships 'promotional coins', giveaways and contests with prizes, plus streaks and leaderboards. The Promotion and Regulation of Online Gaming Act 2025 (passed 20-21 August 2025) bans offering, advertising and facilitating payments for 'online money games' — defined as games where a user pays money or other stakes (including credits, coins and tokens convertible to money) in expectation of monetary or other enrichment, whether the outcome turns on skill, chance or both. Penalties: up to 3 years imprisonment and/or Rs 1 crore for offering; up to 2 years and/or Rs 50 lakh for advertising; up to 3 years and/or Rs 1 crore for facilitating payments. -> FAILS: If coins are ever purchasable, or a paid subscription is a precondition for entering a prize contest, the design edges toward 'stake in expectation of enrichment' — a criminal, not civil, exposure with personal liability for officers. A separate exposure: prize contests decided by chance can attract state lottery/prize-competition law, and Google Play requires developers running contests to publish official rules, fixed winner counts, deadlines and odds for chance-based selection. Apple 3.1.1 requires odds disclosure for any purchased randomised virtual items. -> FIX: Draw a bright line and enforce it in code: coins are NEVER purchasable, NEVER convertible to money or to a monetary-value good, and NEVER required to enter a contest. Earn-only, spend-only-on-in-app cosmetics/unlocks. No randomised paid items at all — no loot boxes, no mystery boxes, no spin-the-wheel behind a paywall — which sidesteps Apple's odds-disclosure regime entirely rather than trying to satisfy it. For giveaways: no purchase necessary, published official rules with a fixed winner count and deadline, skill-based selection criteria (e.g. highest score on a published rubric) rather than random draw, and — critically for a minor user base — prizes delivered to the verified parent, not the student. Get counsel to review the rewards spec against the Online Gaming Act and applicable state prize-competition law BEFORE build. Sources: https://prsindia.org/billtrack/the-promotion-and-regulation-of-online-gaming-bill-2025 , https://developer.apple.com/app-store/review/guidelines/ , https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/9877032?hl=en
- [medium/db] EC-REWARD-02: Coins are granted as a subscription perk or promotion, and the team later wants to expire unused coins, devalue them, or shut the rewards programme down. Apple Guideline 3.1.1: 'Any credits or in-game currencies purchased via in-app purchase may not expire, and you should make sure you have a restore mechanism for any restorable in-app purchases.' -> FAILS: If coins are ever bundled into an IAP subscription SKU, an argument exists that they were 'purchased via in-app purchase' and therefore may not expire — turning a promotional balance into a perpetual liability across the whole user base. Independently, expiring earned rewards without notice is a classic dark pattern under the CCPA guidelines and an accounting liability (deferred revenue) that nobody modelled. -> FIX: Keep promotional coins legally and technically distinct from anything purchased: separate ledger, separate type, granted by the platform as a gratuitous promotional benefit under published terms that state expiry up front. Never bundle a coin grant into an IAP SKU description. Publish the expiry rule at grant time and notify before expiry. Carry a liability line for outstanding coins. Implement a full transaction ledger (never a mutable balance column) so the balance is always derivable and disputes are resolvable. Source: https://developer.apple.com/app-store/review/guidelines/
- [critical/legal] EC-IP-01: ADMIN bulk-uploads NTA JEE Main/Advanced and NEET previous-year papers as the PYQ corpus — the single highest-value content asset in the product. NTA's copyright policy states content is owned and maintained by NTA and reproduction is permitted 'free of charge after taking proper permission from NTA', with source acknowledgement and no derogatory or misleading use. Copyright Act 1957 s.52(1)(i) permits reproduction by a teacher or pupil in the course of instruction, as part of questions to be answered in an examination, and in answers to such questions. -> FAILS: s.52(1)(i) does not cover a commercial company reproducing a third party's examination paper as saleable inventory — the exception is about the act of instruction and examination, not about publishing a question bank for profit. s.52(1)(h) is instructive on Parliament's intent: collections for instructional use are limited to short passages, no more than two from the same author in five years. Building the core content moat on an unlicensed corpus means an injunction can take down the product's primary value proposition; App Store Guideline 5.2 / Play IP policy also allow takedown on a rightsholder complaint without a court ever being involved. -> FIX: Write to NTA for express written permission for the specific reproduction and commercial context — the policy invites this and it is cheap insurance. Meanwhile, build the corpus so it degrades gracefully: tag every question row with `source_type` (nta_pyq / ncert_derived / original_authored / third_party_licensed), `source_ref`, `licence_status` and `licence_evidence_url`. Make licence_status a filter on every serving query so an entire class can be dark-launched or withdrawn without a migration. Prioritise commissioning ORIGINAL questions modelled on PYQ patterns — pattern, difficulty distribution and topic weighting are not copyrightable; the specific expression is. Sources: https://exams.nta.nic.in/copyright-policy/ , https://indiankanoon.org/doc/1013176/
- [high/legal] EC-IP-02: Solutions, theory blurbs and chapter summaries are written by lifting NCERT textbook passages, diagrams and worked examples — standard practice in Indian test prep because NEET is explicitly NCERT-anchored. NCERT issued a press release on 7 April 2024 warning that 'anyone individual or entity who publishes for commercial sale, NCERT textbooks in whole or in part or uses the NCERT textbook content in such publication, without obtaining the copyright permission from NCERT, shall be proceeded against in accordance with the Copyright Act 1957', and directing permission requests to the Publication Division, NCERT. -> FAILS: NCERT has publicly stated it will act, and the wording captures 'in part' and 'uses the NCERT textbook content' — which reaches paraphrase-adjacent reproduction, lifted diagrams and copied worked examples, not just verbatim chapter dumps. Diagrams are the highest-risk category because they are reproduced pixel-identically. Discovery is easy: an image-similarity sweep finds them instantly. -> FIX: Commission original artwork for every diagram — no exceptions, no 'redrawn from NCERT' that is a trace. Reference NCERT by chapter/section pointer ('NCERT Class 12 Physics, Ch. 4.3') rather than reproducing the text; pointers are not infringement and are more useful to students anyway. Where NCERT text is genuinely needed, seek written permission from the Publication Division. Enforce at the admin-upload layer: require a source attestation dropdown on every content upload, block publish without it, and run periodic image-hash and text-similarity audits over the corpus. Source: https://ncert.nic.in/pdf/announcement/notices/Press_Release_Copyright_Infringement-NCERT.pdf
- [high/product] EC-IP-03: ADMIN users — likely contract subject-matter experts — upload questions as photographed images. Some are photographs of Allen/Aakash/FIITJEE/Resonance printed modules or DPPs. The app has an image-upload path into Supabase Storage with no provenance check and no moderation queue. -> FAILS: Direct copyright infringement in the most evidentially damning form: a photograph of a competitor's copyrighted material, with their watermark or page furniture visible, sitting in your paid product. Coaching institutes actively litigate this. Additionally, admin-uploaded images are user-generated content in Apple's framing — Guideline 1.2 requires a method for filtering objectionable material, a reporting mechanism with timely response, ability to block abusive users, and published contact information. An image path with no moderation is also a vector for uploading illegal or off-topic content into a minors' app. -> FIX: (1) Two-stage publishing: admin uploads to a `pending_review` state; a second, senior reviewer approves before it is servable. Enforce via RLS (an author cannot approve their own row) — this is a policy, not a UI convention. (2) Mandatory provenance attestation per upload, retained with the reviewer identity and timestamp; contractually pass IP warranties and indemnity to the SME in their agreement. (3) Automated pre-checks on upload: OCR the image and flag institute names/watermarks; reject images with detectable third-party branding. (4) Ship a student-facing 'report this question' control (satisfies 1.2 and surfaces both IP and error problems) with a published response SLA and published contact details. (5) Strongly prefer rich-text + LaTeX over images — it is searchable, accessible (see EC-A11Y-01), diffable, and it makes provenance auditable in a way a JPEG never is. Source: https://developer.apple.com/app-store/review/guidelines/
- [high/client] EC-A11Y-01: A blind or low-vision NEET aspirant using VoiceOver or TalkBack opens a Physics question containing an integral, a fraction and a subscripted variable. The content is stored as LaTeX and rendered either to an image or via a KaTeX/MathJax WebView inside React Native. React Native has no native math semantic primitive. -> FAILS: Rendered-to-image math is announced as nothing, or as a filename, or as whatever generic accessibilityLabel was set — the question is simply unanswerable. A LaTeX string exposed as an accessibilityLabel is read character-by-character as gibberish ('backslash frac open brace...'). The ARIA math role over non-semantic HTML lacks the semantic detail for accurate announcement. This is not a niche case: it is a total exclusion of a protected class from an exam-preparation product, in a country where NTA provides scribes and accommodations for exactly these candidates. -> FIX: MathML is the only path with real screen-reader support — VoiceOver performs well, JAWS supports speech and braille, and NVDA integrated MathCAT into core via a November 2025 change (removing the separate add-on requirement). Concretely: (a) store questions as LaTeX source, never as an image, for anything you author; (b) render math in a WebView emitting MathML Core (MathJax can output MathML) rather than image or canvas; (c) supply a human-authored plain-language `spoken_text` field per expression as a mandatory admin field and use it as the accessibility fallback where MathML rendering is unavailable; (d) for the legacy image-uploaded corpus, treat missing spoken_text as an accessibility defect and backfill. Note MathML 4's `intent` attribute (Working Draft) will let authors guide screen-reader speech — design the schema so a per-expression intent/spoken override already has a home. TalkBack support for MathML is the weakest link and must be device-tested, not assumed. Sources: https://www.htmhell.dev/adventcalendar/2025/12/ , https://oneuptime.com/blog/post/2026-01-15-react-native-screen-reader-support/view
- [medium/client] EC-A11Y-02: A student sets iOS Dynamic Type or Android font scale to 200%. WCAG 2.2 SC 1.4.4 requires text to resize to 200% without loss of content or functionality; SC 2.5.8 requires 24x24 CSS px minimum target size; SC 1.4.3 requires 4.5:1 contrast for normal text; SC 1.3.4 requires the app not to lock to a single orientation unless essential; SC 2.4.11 requires the focus indicator not to be obscured. W3C published 'Guidance on Applying WCAG 2.2 to Mobile Applications' as a Group Draft Note on 6 May 2025. -> FAILS: Rendered math does not reflow — a KaTeX-rendered equation at 200% overflows horizontally and clips, so the student sees half an integral. A/B/C/D option buttons in a compact grid fall below 24x24. The 'deliberate motion design' bar produces animated transitions that violate prefers-reduced-motion. A sticky test-timer header obscures the focused option (2.4.11). Locking to portrait breaks users with mounted devices or motor impairments. Every one of these is invisible in design review at default settings. -> FIX: Test at 200% text scale as a CI gate, not as a manual pass. Give math its own horizontally-scrollable container so an over-wide expression scrolls inside its box and the page body never scrolls horizontally. Enforce a 44x44 pt minimum on all interactive targets (exceeds the 24x24 floor and matches platform HIG). Honour prefers-reduced-motion / UIAccessibility.isReduceMotionEnabled on every animation — build it into the shared animation primitive so it cannot be forgotten per-component. Support both orientations. Verify contrast in both light and dark themes. Ensure the server-authoritative timer's remaining time is available as an accessibility announcement, not only as a visual element. Source: https://www.w3.org/TR/wcag2mobile-22/
- [medium/legal] EC-A11Y-03: Two separate Indian legal hooks. (1) The Rights of Persons with Disabilities Act 2016 (ss.40, 42, 46) on accessibility standards for ICT; in Rajive Raturi v. Union of India (November 2024) the Supreme Court held Rule 15 of the RPwD Rules 2017 ultra vires for framing accessibility as recommendatory guidelines rather than mandatory standards, and directed the Union to frame enforceable norms. (2) DPDP Rules 2025 Rule 11 requires the verifiable consent of a lawful guardian for a person with disability under lawful guardianship, with confirmation that the guardianship is valid under law (e.g. court-appointed). -> FAILS: Accessibility is drifting from 'good practice' to enforceable obligation, and remediating a shipped React Native app with image-based math is an order of magnitude more expensive than building it right. Separately, Rule 11 is a distinct consent path from Rule 10 that nobody builds: a 20-year-old adult student under lawful guardianship needs guardian consent with guardianship validation — a different verification artefact (court order) than the DigiLocker adult-identity check used for parents of minors. -> FIX: Build to WCAG 2.2 Level AA as the internal bar and state it publicly in an accessibility statement (it is also a procurement differentiator for any future institutional channel). Model consent as a polymorphic relation supporting three bases — self (adult), parent (Rule 10, minor), guardian (Rule 11, PwD) — with a per-basis verification-evidence type, rather than a boolean `parent_consented` column. Track the Union's response to Rajive Raturi for mandatory ICT accessibility standards as they land. Sources: https://www.dpdp.ind.in/rules.php , https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2024/11/12/union-mandate-rules-under-rpwd-act-ensure-accessibility-of-public-places-supreme-court/
- [critical/product] EC-OPS-01: In Sukdeb Saha v. State of Andhra Pradesh (2025 SCC OnLine SC 1515, 25 July 2025) the Supreme Court issued 15 binding guidelines applying to 'all educational institutions across India, including public and private schools, colleges, universities, training centres, coaching institutes, residential academies, and hostels, irrespective of their affiliation'. These include refraining from batch segregation based on academic performance, public shaming and disproportionate academic targets; prominent display of mental-health helpline numbers including Tele-MANAS; written referral protocols; and heightened attention in coaching hubs (Kota, Jaipur, Sikar, Hyderabad, Delhi, Chennai, Mumbai). -> FAILS: The product's core engagement loop — global leaderboards, public rank, streak-loss pressure, 'you dropped 400 ranks' notifications — is the digital form of exactly what the Court told institutions to stop doing to this exact demographic. Whether the guidelines formally bind an online platform is unsettled ('training centres' is broad; edtech is not named), but the reputational and regulatory exposure after a single publicised aspirant suicide linked to the app's ranking mechanics is existential regardless of the technical legal answer. This is also the highest-probability path to emergency feature removal. -> FIX: Design the competitive layer defensively rather than removing it: (a) leaderboards opt-in, pseudonymous, cohort-scoped (self-selected peer group), never global-public-by-default, never showing real names or schools; (b) frame feedback as improvement-relative-to-self (mastery delta, topic gaps) rather than rank-relative-to-others; (c) no loss-aversion mechanics — a broken streak must not be punitive, and never send a shaming or urgency notification; (d) rate-limit and quiet-hours all push notifications (no 1 a.m. re-engagement pings to a 17-year-old); (e) surface Tele-MANAS 14416 and a crisis-support screen persistently in the app, not buried in settings; (f) publish a student-wellbeing policy and have the Terms of Service disclaim any diagnostic or counselling role while pointing to real services. Treat this as a design constraint on the rewards layer at spec time, not a legal review at launch. Source: https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2025/07/25/supreme-court-guidelines-protecting-mental-health-of-coaching-and-college-students-legal-news/
- [high/legal] EC-OPS-02: A parent complains that a test was scored wrongly, or that their child's data was misused, and there is no named officer or published SLA. Three separate grievance regimes may apply: Consumer Protection (E-Commerce) Rules 2020 Rule 4 requires every e-commerce entity to appoint a grievance officer, display their name, contact details and designation on the platform, acknowledge every complaint within 48 hours and redress it within one month; DPDP requires published contact information for data-processing questions (Rule 9) and a rights-exercise/grievance mechanism (Rule 14); the IT (Intermediary Guidelines) Rules 2021 impose grievance-officer duties on intermediaries. -> FAILS: Three regimes, three clocks, one inbox that nobody owns. The 48-hour acknowledgement is the most commonly missed and the easiest to prove against you. On intermediary status: selling your own curated content is publisher activity, not intermediary activity — but the moment students can share notes, post doubts, or the app hosts third-party content, intermediary obligations (including a Grievance Officer resident in India, and takedown timelines) plausibly attach. -> FIX: Appoint ONE named Grievance Officer resident in India covering all three regimes and publish name, designation, email and postal address in-app and on the website (Apple 1.2 also requires published contact information). Build a ticketing system with an enforced 48-hour auto-acknowledgement and a 30-day resolution timer with escalation alerts — do not run this on a shared mailbox. Keep student notes strictly private-to-self at launch to stay clearly outside intermediary status; if social features are ever added, re-run the intermediary analysis first and budget for takedown SLAs. [confidence: medium — whether this app is an 'intermediary' under the IT Rules is a fact-specific legal question and is unverified; the safe read is that a pure first-party content app is not, but any UGC/sharing feature changes the analysis.] Sources: https://www.consumerprotection.in/rule-4-duties-of-e-commerce-entities/ , https://www.dpdp.ind.in/rules.php
- [high/product] EC-OPS-03: Growth ships countdown timers ('offer ends in 02:14:33' that resets on reload), a pre-ticked auto-renew checkbox, a hard-to-find cancel path, 'only 3 seats left in this batch', and streak-guilt copy. The CCPA's Guidelines for Prevention and Regulation of Dark Patterns 2023 enumerate specified dark patterns including false urgency, basket sneaking, subscription trap, confirm shaming, forced action and nagging; CCPA has run enforcement and required leading e-commerce platforms to conduct self-audits and file self-declarations of compliance. Consumer Protection (E-Commerce) Rules 2020 Rule 4 separately prohibits recording consent automatically, 'including in the form of pre-ticked checkboxes', and prohibits cancellation charges on consumers unless the entity bears similar charges. -> FAILS: Growth-team defaults are, almost item for item, the enumerated dark-patterns list. Applied to a minor user base this compounds with DPDPA s.9(2)'s prohibition on processing likely to cause a detrimental effect on a child's wellbeing — manipulative commercial pressure on a stressed 17-year-old is precisely the harm the provision contemplates. Enforcement action here is public and brand-damaging out of proportion to the revenue gained. -> FIX: Hard product rules, enforced in code review: no countdown that is not backed by a real server-side deadline; no pre-ticked consent or auto-renew boxes anywhere (Rule 4 makes this explicit); cancellation reachable in the same number of taps as subscription, in-app; no scarcity claims without real inventory; no confirm-shaming copy ('No thanks, I'll stay unprepared'). Run the CCPA 13-pattern list as an explicit pre-launch checklist and keep the signed-off artefact. Given a child user base, hold a stricter bar than an adult-facing e-commerce app would. Sources: https://consumeraffairs.gov.in/public/upload/admin/cmsfiles/whatsnews/Draft_Guidelines_for_Prevention_and_Regulation_of_Dark_Patterns,_2023_whatsnews.pdf , https://www.consumerprotection.in/rule-4-duties-of-e-commerce-entities/
- [high/legal] EC-BIZ-01: Pricing is set at Rs 999/year with no GST line. Coaching and online test-prep services are taxable at 18% GST under SAC 9992; the Notification 12/2017 education exemption covers only 'educational institutions' — pre-school to higher secondary schools and institutions awarding recognised qualifications — and coaching/test-prep does not qualify because the qualification (Class 12 certificate, JEE rank) is conferred by a separate body. Registration is mandatory above the Rs 20 lakh turnover threshold; mode of delivery (online vs offline) does not change the treatment. -> FAILS: Pricing Rs 999 inclusive means realised revenue is Rs 846.61, an 18% margin hole discovered at the first GST filing. Stacked with Apple/Google commission (15-30%), a Rs 999 iOS subscription nets roughly Rs 590-720 — before payment, content and infrastructure cost. Separately, failing to issue compliant tax invoices with the correct place of supply (B2C: recipient's state, which for a pan-India student base means state-wise output tax reporting) creates a filing mess that is painful to unwind retroactively. -> FIX: Model unit economics as: list price → less store commission → less 18% GST → less PSP fee (web) → net. Decide explicitly whether prices are GST-inclusive or exclusive and say so on every price surface. Automate invoice generation with GSTIN, SAC 9992, place of supply derived from the user's declared state (capture it at signup — it is also needed for state-wise reporting), and sequential invoice numbering. Register for GST before crossing Rs 20 lakh, not after. Do not market the product using 'educational institution' framing in a way that implies an exemption you do not have. [confidence: medium — GST treatment of specific edtech bundles (e.g. content + assessment + counselling) can vary by AAR ruling; get a CA opinion on the exact bundle. Threshold and rate verified.] Source: https://taxclue.in/gst-on-coaching-classes
- [high/legal] EC-BIZ-02: A student's parent cancels two weeks into a six-month test series and demands a full refund, citing content quality. Consumer Protection (E-Commerce) Rules 2020 requires the refund policy to be displayed and refunds to be effected within a reasonable period, and bars cancellation charges unless the entity bears similar charges. The Ministry of Education's Guidelines for Regulation of Coaching Centres (January 2024) additionally require pro-rata refund of fees on mid-course withdrawal and prohibit misleading advertisements and guaranteed-rank/selection claims. App-store purchases add a third layer: Apple and Google handle refunds through their own systems and can refund unilaterally. -> FAILS: Three refund authorities with different rules and no single source of truth for entitlement state. The specific failure: Apple issues a store refund, the user retains full access because the entitlement was never revoked, and the same user re-subscribes on web at a discount. At scale this is a systematic revenue leak. Second failure: marketing copy claiming 'X% of our students cleared JEE' without substantiation is a misleading advertisement under both the coaching guidelines and CCPA rules. -> FIX: Make entitlement server-authoritative and event-driven: consume Apple App Store Server Notifications V2 (REFUND, REVOKE, DID_CHANGE_RENEWAL_STATUS) and Google Play Real-time Developer Notifications, and revoke access on refund automatically — never trust a client receipt. Publish one refund policy covering all three purchase channels with pro-rata terms for direct/web purchases and a clear statement that store purchases follow store policy. Never claim guaranteed ranks or selection; any results claim must be substantiable with named, consented students and must not use a minor's image or name without verified parental consent. [confidence: medium — the January 2024 coaching-centre guidelines' applicability to a purely online platform is unverified; their below-16 enrolment bar and refund/advertising rules are directed at physical coaching centres but signal the regulator's posture toward this market.] Sources: https://www.consumerprotection.in/rule-4-duties-of-e-commerce-entities/ , https://www.thehindu.com/education/ministry-of-education-issues-guidelines-for-coaching-centres-prohibits-intake-of-students-below-16-years/article67751924.ece
